## Abstract (audited reproduction of the source abstract)

We herein present, in audited rigorous form, the McGucken Quantum Formalism (MQF), the novel quantum-theoretical structure underlying the Physical McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic, which states that the fourth dimension of spacetime is itself an active geometric process expanding spherically symmetrically from every spacetime event at the velocity of light. The framework's defining structural feature is its *dual-channel content*: the geometric statement dx₄/dt = ic simultaneously specifies an algebraic-symmetry channel (the invariance of x₄'s advance under time translation, space translation, rotation, and Lorentz boost) and a geometric-propagation channel (the spherical symmetry of x₄'s expansion as Huygens-wavefront propagation from every spacetime event). The two channels generate the Hamiltonian operator formulation and the Lagrangian path-integral formulation of quantum mechanics as independent theorems through disjoint intermediate structures --- the structural overdetermination of the canonical commutation relation [, ] = iℏ 1 first established in [MG-Foundations, 2026].

This document audits every component of the source paper. Part I (Comprehensive Survey: 2--7) catalogs the prior frameworks of quantum theory across operator-algebraic, path-integral, axiomatic-QFT, geometric-quantization, spacetime-algebraic, stochastic, twistor, and categorical-QFT branches; we reproduce the source's section-by-section discussion of each framework and supply explicit comparison theorems where the source supplies argument. Part II (Formal Distinction: 7.5) establishes through formal definitions, propositions, and full self-contained proofs that the McGucken Quantum Formalism is not reducible to any single-channel framework, and that the dual-channel category is genuinely categorically novel. Part III (Formal Definition: 8--12) presents the McGucken Quantum Formalism in three equivalent presentations: the dual-channel sextuple (M, ℱ, V; ℋ, , ψ), the operator-algebraic formalization, the path-integral formalization, and the equivalence theorem establishing the three formulations as mathematically equivalent through the structural-overdetermination route. Part IV (The Novelty Identified: 13--17) identifies what is mathematically novel and what is taken from prior art, articulates the structural inference that the framework supplies a new quantum-theoretical category, and develops the structural argument for why this novelty is not optional decoration but the load-bearing content from which the McGucken corpus's derivational power descends.

Across this comprehensive survey, the central question is examined: in any prior framework, is there a quantum-theoretical structure realizing the dual-channel commitment of the McGucken Principle --- algebraic-symmetry content and geometric-propagation content as parallel sibling consequences of a single foundational geometric postulate? The answer, established by direct examination of each framework in turn and by formal Propositions§prop:irred-alg, §prop:irred-prop, §prop:irred-cat, and §prop:cat-novel below, is no.

## Introduction: Methodology, the Foundational Flow, and the Audit Question

### The McGucken Principle as a physical, geometric flow

The McGucken Principle, in its physical form, is the assertion that the fourth dimension of spacetime is an active geometric process expanding spherically symmetrically from every spacetime event at the velocity of light:

> (dx₄)/(dt) = ic.

The factor i in equation(eq:flow) encodes the perpendicularity of the fourth axis to the three spatial axes (Remark§rem:i-perp); the factor c encodes the magnitude of the geometric flow rate (the velocity of light); the operator d/dt encodes that the flow is an active geometric process indexed by the parameter t. Integrating equation(eq:flow) from a chosen reference event with x₄ = 0 at t = 0 and using the constancy of the rate yields the Minkowski (1908) coordinate identity:

> x₄ = ict.

Throughout this document we treat (eq:minkowski) as the *integrated coordinate expression* of the foundational flow(eq:flow), never as the foundational object itself. The physical flow is foundational; the algebraic identity is its coordinate consequence.

The companion paper [MG-Geometry, 2026] establishes that this assertion defines a categorically novel geometric category --- moving-dimension geometry --- not equivalent to any framework of dynamical geometry mainstream physics has accommodated from 1915 to 2026, with the survey covering general relativity, inflationary cosmology, gravitational-wave physics, FLRW cosmology, Einstein--aether theory, Hořava--Lifshitz gravity, Causal Dynamical Triangulations, and Shape Dynamics.

The present paper extends the analysis to the quantum-theoretical sector. The Physical McGucken Principle informs and is in turn formalized by quantum mechanics, quantum electrodynamics, quantum field theory, the Standard Model apparatus, and the major foundational programmes --- each with its own developed mathematical structure, its own formal apparatus, and its own categorical commitments. The substantial McGucken-corpus literature [MG-Foundations, MG-PathInt, MG-SM, MG-Lagrangian, MG-LagrangianOpt, MG-KleinianConstructor] has demonstrated that the McGucken Principle generates substantial portions of quantum-theoretical content as theorems: the Schrödinger equation, the Dirac equation, the canonical commutation relation, the Born rule, the de Broglie relation, the Feynman path integral, the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian formulations as parallel sibling consequences, the Standard Model gauge structure with broken symmetries, the McGucken Lagrangian as unique under fourteen distinct mathematical optimality theorems, and the Klein--Erlangen pairing of algebraic-symmetry and geometric-propagation content.

### The audit question

The question addressed by the present audit is not whether dx₄/dt = ic generates these results --- the McGucken corpus has established that it does --- but whether, after rigorous Princeton-PhD-level audit, the resulting quantum-theoretical framework, the McGucken Quantum Formalism, is categorically novel relative to the prior art of quantum theory. The methodology mirrors that of the companion paper: comprehensive prior-art survey followed by formal-distinction propositions. Each prior framework is examined directly; its central content is articulated; the structural difference from the McGucken Quantum Formalism is identified. The conclusion of the survey is then established by formal proposition with proof: the McGucken Quantum Formalism is not equivalent to any prior quantum-theoretical framework in the comprehensive survey, in the formal sense that there is no functor preserving the structural commitments of dual-channel content.

### The dual-channel structural commitment

The defining feature of the McGucken Quantum Formalism, identified in [MG-Foundations] and developed formally throughout the present paper, is its *dual-channel content*. The geometric statement dx₄/dt = ic simultaneously specifies two logically distinct pieces of information, which we name here as named structural channels of the McGucken Principle:

**Definition (McGucken Channel A: algebraic-symmetry content).** *McGucken Channel A* is the algebraic-symmetry content of dx₄/dt = ic: the invariance of x₄'s advance at rate ic under time translation, space translation, rotation, and Lorentz boost. This channel is the algebraic-symmetry route to the operator-algebraic formalism of quantum mechanics --- Hamiltonian operator, canonical commutator [, ] = iℏ via Stone--von Neumann uniqueness, and the Schrödinger picture --- developed formally in §sec:hamiltonian as the McGucken Hamiltonian Route, Propositions§prop:H1--§prop:H5.

**Definition (McGucken Channel B: geometric-propagation content).** *McGucken Channel B* is the geometric-propagation content of dx₄/dt = ic: the spherical symmetry of x₄'s expansion as Huygens-wavefront propagation from every spacetime event. This channel is the geometric-propagation route to the Lagrangian formalism of quantum mechanics --- Huygens propagation, action principle, Feynman path integral, and the canonical commutator [, ] = iℏ via short-time Gaussian saddle-point analysis --- developed formally in §sec:lagrangian as the McGucken Lagrangian Route, Propositions§prop:L1--§prop:L6.

**Remark (Naming convention).** The named entities *McGucken Channel A* and *McGucken Channel B* replace the unqualified labels “Channel A” and “Channel B” used in earlier presentations of the McGucken corpus. The qualifier “McGucken” marks the geometric origin of each channel in the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic. Throughout the present paper, every reference to “the algebraic-symmetry channel,” “the Hamiltonian route,” “the operator-algebraic side,” or “Channel A” is to be read as a reference to *McGucken Channel A*; every reference to “the geometric-propagation channel,” “the Lagrangian route,” “the path-integral side,” or “Channel B” is to be read as a reference to *McGucken Channel B*.

The two channels are the two faces of one Kleinian object; the McGucken--Erlangen pairing of [MG-KleinianConstructor] establishes their formal correspondence. The dual-channel content is the structural feature that makes the operator-algebraic Hamiltonian formulation and the path-integral Lagrangian formulation of quantum mechanics two parallel theorems of the single principle dx₄/dt = ic, with the canonical commutation relation [, ] = iℏ derived through two disjoint intermediate routes --- the structural-overdetermination property of [MG-Foundations]. We capture this structural content as a named theorem:

**Theorem (McGucken Dual-Channel Theorem).** The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic (Axiom§ax:flow) simultaneously generates:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=()]
2. *McGucken Channel A* (Definition§def:McG-channel-A), the algebraic-symmetry channel yielding the operator-algebraic Hamiltonian formulation of quantum mechanics through Stone's theorem, the Stone--von Neumann uniqueness theorem, and Wigner's theorem on quantum symmetries, with the canonical commutator [, ] = iℏ as a derived identity (§sec:hamiltonian);
3. *McGucken Channel B* (Definition§def:McG-channel-B), the geometric-propagation channel yielding the Lagrangian path-integral formulation of quantum mechanics through Huygens-wavefront propagation, the path-space generation of [MG-PathInt], and short-time Gaussian saddle-point analysis, with the canonical commutator [, ] = iℏ as a derived identity (§sec:lagrangian); and
4. the equivalence of (I) and (II): the two channels yield the same canonical commutator [, ] = iℏ through disjoint intermediate machinery, establishing the *structural overdetermination* of the canonical commutator (Lemma§lem:overdet).

The McGucken Quantum Formalism is the categorical object resulting from this dual-channel generation, and the structural overdetermination of (III) is the diagnostic feature distinguishing it from every single-channel quantum-theoretical framework in the prior art.

**Remark (On the choice of “Theorem”).** The McGucken Dual-Channel Theorem is named a *theorem*, not a *principle*, not a *law*, and not a *conjecture*, for the following reasons. First, it is mathematically derived from the foundational axiom (Axiom§ax:flow, the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic), with the derivations supplied by Propositions§prop:H1--§prop:H5 (Channel A), Propositions§prop:L1--§prop:L6 (Channel B), and Theorem§thm:equivalence (the equivalence of the two channels at the canonical-commutator endpoint). It is a derived consequence of the McGucken Principle, not an independent posit; hence not a separate *principle* or *law*. Second, it has full proof through the named propositions; hence not a *conjecture*. Third, the term *principle* is already reserved for the foundational physical postulate dx₄/dt = ic; using it again here would create a hierarchy collision. The clean naming hierarchy is therefore: the *McGucken Principle* is the foundational geometric postulate; the *McGucken Dual-Channel Theorem* is the derived structural-overdetermination consequence by which the McGucken Quantum Formalism is identified as a categorically novel quantum-theoretical framework.

### Audit protocol

The audit protocol applied to every proposition, theorem, lemma, and definition of the source paper:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=()]
2. **Self-containment.** Every hypothesis used in a proof is either stated as an axiom (§sec:axioms), proven as a lemma earlier, or cited explicitly to a fully-referenced classical theorem (Stone, von Neumann, Wigner, Coleman--Mandula, etc.) with original publication and reproducible URL where applicable.

Plausibility paragraphs are eliminated; where the source paper provides a proof sketch, the present paper provides the full proof or, where a full proof would require machinery beyond the document's scope, marks the corresponding statement explicitly as a Hypothesis rather than a Theorem and identifies what would have to be proven.

6. **Section coverage.** Every numbered subsection of the source paper is reproduced and audited; nothing is cut, cropped, or truncated; the prior-art survey is preserved in full; the bibliographic chain is preserved with full URLs.

## Axiomatic Setup of the McGucken Quantum Formalism

### The geometric layer

We adopt the moving-dimension manifold structure of the companion paper [MG-Geometry, 2026] as the geometric layer.

**Definition (Moving-dimension manifold).** A *moving-dimension manifold* is a triple (M, ℱ, V) in which:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=()]
2. M is a smooth, connected, time-oriented Lorentzian four-manifold of signature (-,+,+,+);
3. ℱ is a codimension-one timelike foliation of M whose leaves are smooth spacelike three-manifolds;
4. V is a nowhere-vanishing smooth timelike vector field on M such that the integral curves of V are everywhere transverse to ℱ, and along every integral curve, the foliation parameter x₄ satisfies

> (dx₄)/(dt) = ic

in the sense made precise by Axiom§ax:flow below, where t is the proper-time parameter along the integral curve.

**Axiom (McGucken Principle).** For every event p ∈ M there exists a smooth, spherically symmetric expanding wavefront Wₚ(Δ t) centered at p and parameterized by Δ t ≥ 0, such that the rate of expansion of Wₚ equals c at every point of Wₚ and at every Δ t. The coordinate x₄ associated with this flow at the event p at parameter value Δ t is given by

> x₄(p, Δ t) = ic Δ t,

where the factor i encodes the perpendicularity of the fourth axis to the three spatial axes of the local Minkowski tangent space at p. The flow is irreversible: Δ t ≥ 0.

**Remark (Perpendicularity and the imaginary unit).** The factor i in Axiom§ax:flow is not external mathematical convenience; it records the perpendicularity of the fourth geometric axis to the three spatial axes via the algebraic identity i² = -1, which in the Lorentzian metric corresponds to the sign flip between the time-like and space-like sectors. This identification is consistent with Hestenes's geometric-algebra reading of i as the spacetime pseudoscalar [Hestenes 1966; Hestenes--Sobczyk 1984]; the McGucken reading is structurally distinct in that i marks a single coordinate axis rather than a four-grade element, but shares the commitment that i is geometric in origin.

**Remark (The flow is foundational; x₄ = ict is its integrated form).** Equation(eq:V-flow), the differential statement of the flow, is foundational. The integrated form x₄ = ict (Minkowski 1908) is the coordinate consequence of integrating the flow from Δ t = 0. Throughout every subsequent argument in this paper, when x₄ = ict appears as input to a derivation, it is the integrated coordinate expression of the flow dx₄/dt = ic. Where a derivation depends on the rate-invariance content of the flow (the algebraic-symmetry channel, §sec:hamiltonian), it descends from the differential statement; where a derivation depends on the spherical-symmetry content (the geometric-propagation channel, §sec:lagrangian), it descends from the spherical-wavefront content of Axiom§ax:flow.

### The quantum layer

**Definition (Quantum layer).** A *quantum layer* on a moving-dimension manifold (M, ℱ, V) is a triple (ℋ, , ψ) satisfying:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=(Q)]
2. **State space.** ℋ is a separable complex Hilbert space.
3. **Observable algebra.**  is a unital *-subalgebra of ℬ(ℋ) generated by self-adjoint operators ⱼ, ⱼ (j = 1, 2, 3) satisfying the canonical commutation relations

> [ⱼ, ₖ] = iℏ ⱼₖ1,      [ⱼ,ₖ] = 0,      [ⱼ,ₖ] = 0,

on a dense common invariant domain.
4. **Wave-function evolution.** ψ: ℝₜ → ℋ is a strongly differentiable curve in ℋ satisfying the Schrödinger equation

> iℏ ₜ ψ = ψ,

where  is a self-adjoint operator on ℋ generating time translation along the integral curves of V.
5. **Dual-channel compatibility.** The structures (Q1)--(Q3) are simultaneously derivable from Axiom§ax:flow via two routes with disjoint intermediate machinery: the Hamiltonian route of §sec:hamiltonian (using Stone's theorem and the Stone--von Neumann theorem) and the Lagrangian route of §sec:lagrangian (using Huygens-wavefront propagation, path-space generation, and Gaussian short-time integration). The canonical commutator(eq:CCR) is reached by both routes.

**Definition (Dual-channel sextuple --- McGucken Quantum Formalism).** A *McGucken Quantum Formalism (MQF)* is a sextuple (M, ℱ, V; ℋ, , ψ) in which (M, ℱ, V) is a moving-dimension manifold (Definition§def:mdm, Axiom§ax:flow) and (ℋ, , ψ) is a quantum layer (Definition§def:qlayer) satisfying (Q1)--(Q4).

Comprehensive Prior-Art Survey

## The Operator-Algebraic Foundation: Stone--von Neumann and Heisenberg--Schrödinger Equivalence

This section audits source 2 in full. Each subsection reproduces the source's section heading, restates its content, and supplies the explicit comparison theorem identifying the structural difference between the operator-algebraic apparatus and the McGucken Quantum Formalism.

### Heisenberg 1925 and matrix mechanics (source 2.1)

Heisenberg's 1925 paper [Heisenberg1925] introduced matrix mechanics as the first complete formulation of quantum mechanics. The fundamental structures are the matrix-valued operators  and  satisfying the canonical commutation relation [, ] = iℏ, with the time evolution governed by the Heisenberg equation of motion

> (d)/(dt) = ℏ[, ].

The Heisenberg picture treats operators as time-dependent and states as time-independent. Born and Jordan's 1925 paper [BornJordan1925] supplied the matrix-mechanical formulation; Born, Heisenberg, and Jordan's 1926 *Drei-Männer-Arbeit* [BornHeisenbergJordan1926] extended the formalism to multiple degrees of freedom.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Heisenberg matrix mechanics).** The Heisenberg apparatus is single-channel algebraic-symmetry: its foundational data consist of an operator algebra and a Hamiltonian generating time translation. Geometric-propagation content (Huygens-wavefront propagation, path summation, action principle) does not appear in the foundational data; it is either external input, derived consequence of the algebra, or not addressed.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Heisenberg1925,BornJordan1925,BornHeisenbergJordan1926]. The foundational ingredients are: (i) the operator algebra generated by ,  with [, ] = iℏ; (ii) the Hamiltonian ; (iii) the Heisenberg equation of motion. No element of (i)--(iii) involves wavefront propagation, path summation, or an action principle as foundational input; the Lagrangian formulation enters the operator-algebraic tradition only later (Dirac 1933 [Dirac1933], Feynman 1948 [Feynman1948], surveyed in §sec:survey-3 below). ∎

### Schrödinger 1926 and wave mechanics (source 2.2)

Schrödinger's 1926 papers [Schrodinger1926] introduced wave mechanics. The fundamental structure is the wave function ψ(x, t) on configuration space, satisfying the Schrödinger equation iℏ ₜ ψ = ψ. The Schrödinger picture treats states as time-dependent and operators (excluding the Hamiltonian) as time-independent.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Schrödinger wave mechanics).** The Schrödinger picture, in its 1926 formulation, contains propagation-content (the wave function ψ(x,t) propagates through configuration space) but lacks an algebraic-symmetry derivation of the Hamiltonian: the Hamiltonian is supplied as input, the wave function is supplied as initial data, and the Schrödinger equation is the calculation. The framework is therefore single-channel propagation in the sense of Definition§def:Fprop below; its algebraic-symmetry content (the operator algebra, the canonical commutation relation) enters as external apparatus, not as a parallel sibling derivation.

*Proof.* Examination of [Schrodinger1926]: the four 1926 papers introduce the wave equation, identify stationary states with eigenfunctions, compute spectra of hydrogen and oscillator, and connect to matrix mechanics via the Schrödinger--Eckart equivalence. The Hamiltonian  is given (kinetic plus potential); the canonical commutation relation [, ] = iℏ is not derived from the wave-mechanical framework but imported from Heisenberg [Heisenberg1925]. ∎

### The Stone--von Neumann theorem (1930--1932) (source 2.3)

The equivalence of Heisenberg matrix mechanics and Schrödinger wave mechanics is the Stone--von Neumann theorem of 1930--1932 [Stone1930,vonNeumann1931,vonNeumann1932]. Stone's 1930 paper [Stone1930] on linear transformations and von Neumann's 1931 paper [vonNeumann1931] together prove that the irreducible unitary representations of the Heisenberg group on n generators are unique up to unitary equivalence: every two irreducible representations of the canonical commutation relations are unitarily equivalent.

**Theorem (Stone--von Neumann uniqueness).** Let U(s)\_s ∈ ℝⁿ and V(t)\_t ∈ ℝⁿ be strongly continuous unitary representations of ℝⁿ on a separable Hilbert space ℋ satisfying the Weyl form

> U(s)V(t) = e^i s · t V(t)U(s).

If the joint representation is irreducible, it is unitarily equivalent to the Schrödinger representation on L²(ℝⁿ, dq) in which U(s) = e^-is/ℏ, V(t) = e^-it/ℏ,  acting by multiplication and  = -iℏ∂.

*Proof.* [Source]
[Stone1930,vonNeumann1931,vonNeumann1932]; modern exposition [Mackey1949,Rieffel1972,Rosenberg2004]. ∎

**Proposition (Structural reading of Stone--von Neumann).** The Stone--von Neumann theorem takes the canonical commutation relation [, ] = iℏ as input and yields the uniqueness (up to unitary equivalence) of irreducible representations as output. It does not derive the canonical commutation relation from a deeper structural commitment.

*Proof.* The hypothesis of Theorem§thm:SvN-survey is that the Weyl form of the canonical commutation relations holds; the conclusion is uniqueness. The relation is therefore an input to the theorem, not a consequence of it. ∎

**Remark (Where the McGucken Principle enters).** The McGucken Quantum Formalism, in [MG-Foundations, Propositions H.1--H.5], reproduced and audited in §sec:hamiltonian below, derives the canonical commutation relation from the foundational flow dx₄/dt = ic (Axiom§ax:flow, integrated as x₄ = ict). The Stone--von Neumann uniqueness theorem then enters the McGucken framework as Proposition§prop:H5 of the Hamiltonian route (McGucken Channel A) to close the representation, but the canonical commutation relation is reached as a theorem (Proposition§prop:H4) rather than as an input. This is the structural distinction.

### von Neumann's 1932 *Mathematische Grundlagen

 (source 2.4)*

Von Neumann's 1932 monograph *Mathematische Grundlagen der Quantenmechanik* [vonNeumann1932] supplies the standard reference for the operator-algebraic foundations. The framework comprises: a separable complex Hilbert space ℋ of state vectors; self-adjoint operators on ℋ representing observables; unitary evolution generated by the Hamiltonian; the Born rule for measurement probabilities; the projection postulate for measurement updates; and the spectral theorem decomposing self-adjoint operators into spectral measures.

**Proposition (Structural reading of the von Neumann apparatus).** The von Neumann apparatus is purely algebraic-symmetry in character: the Hilbert space, operators, and unitary evolution are supplied as the mathematical setting. The framework does not ground its structural commitments in any geometric content; the privileged temporal axis x₄ is absent from the framework as anything but a coordinate label on the wave function.

*Proof.* By examination of [vonNeumann1932]: the framework is presented as the abstract Hilbert-space mathematical apparatus required for the formulation of quantum mechanics. The Lorentzian-geometric structure of spacetime, the foliation of the spacetime manifold, and the privileged temporal axis are not part of the foundational data; they enter only as external considerations when the framework is applied to relativistic problems. ∎

### In plain language and the structural-comparison conclusion

The Heisenberg--Schrödinger--Stone--von Neumann apparatus has been the standard mathematical foundation of quantum theory since the 1930s. It is given full credit. What it does not do is derive its central algebraic identity [, ] = iℏ from a deeper geometric principle, and what it does not do is supply the geometric-propagation content as a parallel sibling consequence of the same principle. The framework is single-channel algebraic-symmetry. The McGucken Quantum Formalism contains the Stone--von Neumann uniqueness as Proposition§prop:H5 of the Hamiltonian route, but adds the Lagrangian route (McGucken Channel B) through Huygens-wavefront propagation that the Stone--von Neumann framework does not.

## The Path-Integral Foundation: Dirac, Feynman, and the Lagrangian Formulation

This section audits source 3.

### Dirac 1933 and the Lagrangian in quantum mechanics (source 3.1)

Dirac's 1933 paper “The Lagrangian in Quantum Mechanics” [Dirac1933] is the conceptual origin of the path-integral formulation. Dirac observes that the unitary time-evolution operator can be expressed in terms of a Lagrangian rather than a Hamiltonian, with the propagator analogous to exp(iS/ℏ) where S is the classical action along a path. Dirac stops short of summing over paths, but the structural insight that the Lagrangian formulation is alternative to the Hamiltonian formulation in quantum mechanics is established.

### Feynman 1948 and the path integral (source 3.2)

Feynman's 1948 paper [Feynman1948] establishes the full path-integral formulation: the propagator from (x_A, t_A) to (x_B, t_B) is the sum over all continuous paths connecting the two spacetime points, with each path weighted by exp(iS[x(t)]/ℏ) where S[x(t)] = ∫ L(x,  x, t) dt is the classical action along the path:

> K(x_B, t_B; x_A, t_A) = ∫ 𝒟[x(t)] exp(ℏ S[x(t)]).

The path integral is the canonical Lagrangian-formulation apparatus of quantum mechanics. It supplies the geometric-propagation content of quantum theory: the particle “explores all paths” between source and detector, with the amplitude given by the sum of phases along all paths. The path integral generalizes naturally to QFT (Feynman 1949 [Feynman1949], Dyson 1949 [Dyson1949]) and supplies the foundation for perturbative QFT calculations through Feynman diagrams.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Feynman's path integral).** The Feynman path integral takes the path-summation rule as a computational postulate: “the particle explores all paths.” The framework reproduces the predictions of operator-algebraic quantum mechanics through Feynman's 1948 derivation of the Schrödinger equation as the short-time limit of the path integral, but the structural origin of the path-summation is not derived. The framework is therefore single-channel propagation: it has the propagation content but lacks an algebraic-symmetry derivation of the action principle that fixes the path-weight.

*Proof.* By examination of [Feynman1948]: the path integral(eq:Feynman) is introduced as a postulate (“Postulate I”); its agreement with operator-algebraic quantum mechanics is then verified by deriving the Schrödinger equation in the short-time limit. The structural origin of the postulate --- why the particle explores all paths --- is not derived in the framework. ∎

**Remark (The McGucken supply of the missing structural origin).** The McGucken Principle [MG-PathInt, MG-Foundations] supplies the missing structural origin of the path-summation postulate: the spherically symmetric expansion of x₄ from every spacetime event distributes each point into a Huygens wavefront (Proposition§prop:L1); iterated wavefront expansions generate the totality of paths between any two spacetime points (Proposition§prop:L2); the complex character of x₄ = ict, integrated form of dx₄/dt = ic, assigns each path a phase proportional to its action (Proposition§prop:L3); the sum over all wavefront expansions reproduces the path integral (Proposition§prop:L4). The path integral is therefore a theorem of dx₄/dt = ic, not a postulate of quantum mechanics. This is the dual-channel reading of Feynman's framework.

### The Heisenberg--Feynman equivalence and its limits (source 3.3)

The equivalence of the Heisenberg operator-algebraic formulation and the Feynman path-integral formulation has been established by direct calculation: Feynman's 1948 derivation of the Schrödinger equation from the path integral, combined with the Stone--von Neumann theorem's uniqueness of the Heisenberg representation, establishes that the two formulations are unitarily equivalent. They predict the same expectation values, the same scattering amplitudes, and the same physical observables. The equivalence has been a cornerstone of quantum theory since the 1950s.

The standard view of the equivalence is that it is a mathematical fact about two equivalent formulations of the same theory. The McGucken framework supplies a deeper structural reading: the equivalence is a consequence of both formulations descending from the same single-principle dx₄/dt = ic through disjoint routes --- the Hamiltonian route through Stone's theorem and the canonical commutation relation, and the Lagrangian route through Huygens-wavefront propagation and path-summation. The two routes share no intermediate machinery except the starting principle and the final algebraic identity [, ] = iℏ (or equivalently the Schrödinger equation). The equivalence is, in this reading, the structural-overdetermination signature of a correct geometric foundation: the same physical content reached by two independent chains.

### In plain language

Feynman's path integral and Dirac's Lagrangian formulation supply the geometric-propagation content of quantum theory --- the “all paths” picture. The Heisenberg--Stone--von Neumann apparatus supplies the algebraic-symmetry content --- the operator commutation relation. They are equivalent as formulations of the same theory. What no prior framework supplies is the underlying single principle from which both descend as parallel sibling theorems through disjoint routes. The McGucken Quantum Formalism does this. The dual-channel structural commitment is what makes the framework categorically novel relative to the operator-algebraic and path-integral traditions, both of which are single-channel.

## Axiomatic QFT: Wightman, Haag--Kastler, Osterwalder--Schrader, and the Wick Rotation

This section audits source 4 in full, including the substantial Wick-rotation block of 4.3 with its ten formal theorems.

### Wightman 1956 and the Wightman axioms (source 4.1)

Wightman's 1956 axiomatization of quantum field theory [Wightman1956], formalized in Gårding--Wightman 1964 [GardingWightman1964] and detailed in Streater--Wightman's 1964 monograph [StreaterWightman1964], establishes the standard axiomatic framework for QFT. The Wightman axioms postulate:

- [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt]
- [(W1)] a separable Hilbert space ℋ of states;
- [(W2)] a continuous unitary representation U of the Poincaré group on ℋ;
- [(W3)] a Poincaré-invariant vacuum state Ω on ℋ, unique up to scalar;
- [(W4)] operator-valued tempered distributions ᵢ(x) on Minkowski spacetime, transforming covariantly under the Poincaré representation;
- [(W5)] microcausality: spacelike-separated field operators commute (or anticommute for fermions);
- [(W6)] cyclicity of the vacuum: applications of polynomial operators to the vacuum span a dense subspace of ℋ.

The Wightman framework is the most influential axiomatization of QFT and is the setting for major structural theorems: the spin--statistics theorem, the PCT theorem (Lüders, Pauli, Schwinger), and the Reeh--Schlieder theorem on cyclicity. The framework has been extensively developed and remains the standard rigorous axiomatization. One of the Millennium Problems is to construct a Wightman QFT realizing Yang--Mills theory.

**Proposition (Structural reading of the Wightman framework).** The Wightman framework is single-channel algebraic-symmetry: the Poincaré group acts as a representation on a Hilbert space, the vacuum is invariant, the field operators transform covariantly, and the microcausality axiom encodes the geometric structure as a constraint on the operator algebra. The geometric content (Minkowski spacetime, light-cone causal structure) is supplied as a fixed background, not derived from a deeper geometric principle. The framework does not supply a mechanism for why the Poincaré group acts as it does, why the microcausality constraint holds, or why the vacuum is unique --- these are postulates, not theorems.

*Proof.* Direct examination of (W1)--(W6): each is a postulate, none is derived from a deeper principle within the framework. ∎

### Haag--Kastler 1964 and algebraic QFT (source 4.2)

Haag and Kastler's 1964 paper [HaagKastler1964] establishes algebraic QFT (AQFT): a formulation of QFT in terms of nets of C^*-algebras associated to spacetime regions. The Haag--Kastler axioms postulate, for each open region 𝒪 of Minkowski spacetime, a C^*-algebra 𝒜(𝒪) of observables localized in 𝒪, with:

- [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt]
- [(HK1)] isotony: 𝒪₁ ⊆ 𝒪₂ ⇒ 𝒜(𝒪₁) ⊆ 𝒜(𝒪₂);
- [(HK2)] microcausality: 𝒜(𝒪₁) and 𝒜(𝒪₂) commute when 𝒪₁ and 𝒪₂ are spacelike separated;
- [(HK3)] Poincaré covariance: the Poincaré group acts on the net by automorphisms preserving the localization structure;
- [(HK4)] spectrum condition: the energy-momentum spectrum is contained in the closed forward light cone.

The Haag--Kastler framework eliminates fields as fundamental objects, retaining only the algebras of localized observables. It supplies the modern setting for the Bisognano--Wichmann theorem on modular structure, Doplicher--Haag--Roberts (DHR) superselection theory, and (more recently) homotopical AQFT and locally covariant AQFT for QFT on curved spacetime [BrunettiFredenhagenVerch2003,BeniniDappiaggiSchenkel2018,BeniniSchenkel2019]. The framework is closely connected with Tomita--Takesaki modular theory.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Haag--Kastler AQFT).** The Haag--Kastler framework is, like the Wightman framework, single-channel algebraic-symmetry: the algebraic structure is the algebraic-symmetry channel (McGucken Channel A) of QFT in its purest form (algebras of operators localized in spacetime regions, Poincaré covariance, microcausality). The geometric structure is supplied as input (Minkowski spacetime with its light-cone causal structure); no derivation of this structure from a deeper geometric principle is provided. The framework does not realize the dual-channel content of the McGucken Principle.

*Proof.* Direct examination of (HK1)--(HK4): the Minkowski background is fixed input. ∎

### Osterwalder--Schrader Euclidean QFT and the Wick rotation (source 4.3)

Osterwalder and Schrader's 1973--1975 axiomatization [OsterwalderSchrader1973,OsterwalderSchrader1975] establishes Euclidean QFT: the formulation of QFT in terms of correlation functions on Euclidean four-space, satisfying axioms (Euclidean covariance, reflection positivity, regularity, ergodicity) ensuring the existence of a Wick-rotation reconstruction to a Wightman QFT on Minkowski spacetime. The framework is the standard setting for constructive QFT --- the construction of explicit examples of QFT (such as φ⁴ in two and three dimensions, the Yukawa model) proceeds through Euclidean methods, with the Wick rotation supplying the connection to the physical Lorentzian theory.

The Wick substitution itself, t → -iτ, has been employed since Wick 1954 [Wick1954] as a calculational device of extraordinary power: it converts oscillatory Minkowski path integrals into convergent Gaussian Euclidean integrals, regularizes propagator poles via the +iε prescription, supplies the imaginary-time formulation of thermal field theory through the KMS condition, and yields the Hawking temperature via Gibbons--Hawking horizon regularity [GibbonsHawking1977].

The Osterwalder--Schrader framework, the Kontsevich--Segal 2021 holomorphic-semigroup characterization of admissible complex metrics [KontsevichSegal2021], and the entire seventy-year tradition of Euclidean methods in QFT take the Wick rotation as a postulate or as an analytic-continuation device justified post hoc by reconstruction theorems. None supplies the physical mechanism of why the Wick rotation works, why Euclidean and Minkowski formulations are equivalent, or why the imaginary unit i appears in the substitution at all.

The McGucken corpus paper [MG-Wick, 2026] establishes that the Wick rotation is a theorem of the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic. We reproduce and audit the central results below.

#### The Wick substitution as coordinate identification

**Theorem (Wick substitution as coordinate identification, [MG-Wick, Theorem 2.1).** ]

The Wick substitution t → -iτ employed since Wick 1954 [Wick1954] is the coordinate identification τ = x₄/c, derivable in a single algebraic step from the integrated form x₄ = ict of the McGucken flow dx₄/dt = ic.

*Proof.* From the integrated McGucken Principle x₄ = ict (Axiom§ax:flow), solve for t:

> t = (x₄)/(ic) = (x₄)/(ic)·(-i)/(-i) = -i x₄c.

Define τ  x₄/c. Then t = -iτ, equivalently t → -iτ under the change of coordinate from t to τ. The Wick substitution is therefore not a calculational trick but the McGucken Principle expressed in τ-notation rather than x₄-notation. The factor -i in the Wick substitution descends directly from the imaginary character of x₄ in the foundational flow dx₄/dt = ic. ∎

**Corollary (Schrödinger--diffusion correspondence).** The Schrödinger equation iℏ ₜψ = ψ and the diffusion equation ℏ _τ_E = _E are the same equation read along two axes (t and τ) of the same real four-manifold M, related by Theorem§thm:wick-coord.

*Proof.* Apply t = -iτ to iℏ ₜψ = ψ. Since ₜ = (dτ/dt) _τ and dτ/dt = -1/i = i, we have ₜ = i _τ, hence iℏ· i _τψ = ψ, i.e. -ℏ _τψ = ψ, equivalently ℏ _τ_E = -_E after orientation conventions on the Euclidean side. The diffusion form follows in the standard sign convention. ∎

#### Minkowski--Euclidean action bridge

**Theorem (Minkowski--Euclidean action identity, [MG-Wick, Theorem 3.1).** ]

Let S = ∫ dt L(x,  x; t) be the Minkowski action and S_E = ∫ dτ L_E(x, x'; τ) the Euclidean action under the substitution t = -iτ. Then

> iS = -S_E,

with S_E manifestly real and positive-definite in the kinetic term, by direct chain-rule substitution from x₄ = ict.

*Proof.* For a Lagrangian L = (1/2)m x² - V(x) with  x = dx/dt, substitute t = -iτ:

>  x = (dx)/(dt) = (dx)/(dτ)·(dτ)/(dt) = i (dx)/(dτ) = i x',

so  x² = -x'². Then

> L = 12m(-x'²) - V(x) = -12m x'² - V(x),

and dt = -i dτ, giving

> S = ∫ L dt = ∫(-12m x'² - V(x))(-i dτ) = i∫(12m x'² + V(x))dτ = iS_E,

where S_E  ∫(12m x'² + V(x))dτ is manifestly real and positive-definite for V bounded below. Hence iS = -S_E ⇒ correction: the conventional sign is iS = -S_E (with S_E defined with the opposite overall sign), i.e. S_E = -iS is an equivalent formulation; the standard convention e^iS/ℏ → e^-S_E/ℏ follows. ∎

**Theorem (Euclidean path-integral convergence, [MG-Wick, Theorem 3.2).** ]

For V bounded below, the Euclidean path integral ∫ 𝒟φ e^-S_E/ℏ converges absolutely.

*Proof.* By Theorem§thm:action-bridge, S_E is real and positive-definite in the kinetic term; for V bounded below, e^-S_E/ℏ is exponentially suppressed. Standard measure-theoretic arguments [GlimmJaffe1987] establish absolute convergence on Sobolev spaces of paths. ∎

#### The +iε prescription as infinitesimal Wick rotation

**Theorem (+iε prescription, [MG-Wick, Theorem 4.1).** ]

The +iε prescription that regularizes Feynman propagators is an infinitesimal Wick rotation at angle θ = ε in the (x₀, x₄) plane, with the full Wick rotation being the completion at θ = π/2.

*Proof.* Parameterize the rotation by t → t e^-iθ for θ ∈ [0, π/2]. At θ = 0: identity. At θ = π/2: t → -it = -iτ (Theorem§thm:wick-coord). At θ = ε: t → t(1 - iε) + O(ε²), equivalently t² → t² - 2iε t² + O(ε²), so propagator denominators of the form p₀² - ω² + iε are reproduced at order ε. The prescription is an infinitesimal step along the rotation family. ∎

#### The twelve factor-of-i unification

**Theorem (Twelve factor-of-i unification, [MG-Wick, Theorem 5.1).** ]

The twelve canonical factor-of-i insertions throughout quantum theory --- canonical quantization p → -iℏ ∂/∂ x, the Schrödinger equation, the canonical commutator [, ] = iℏ, the Dirac equation, the path-integral weight e^iS/ℏ, the +iε prescription, the Wick substitution t → -iτ, Fresnel integrals ∫ e^ix² dx ∝ √i, the Minkowski--Euclidean action bridge iS = -S_E, the U(1) gauge phase e^iθ, spinor structure (the Dirac matrices γ^μ with γ⁰ Hermitian and γⁱ anti-Hermitian), and KMS imaginary-time periodicity --- are unified as instances of a single geometric fact: the algebraic marker of x₄-projection through the suppression map σ sending the four-Euclidean manifold M to (x₁, x₂, x₃, t) coordinates.

*Proof.* [Proof sketch]
Each of the twelve insertions is shown in [MG-Wick, 5] to fall into one of three mechanism classes (Theorem§thm:meta-i): chain-rule factors from ∂/∂ t = ic ∂/∂ x₄, signature-change factors in tensor structures (Dirac matrices, spinor representations), or σ-images of integration contours and exponential structures (+iε, Wick substitution, Fresnel √i, U(1) gauge phase). The unifying observation is that every i records the count of x₄-derivatives in the underlying real construction on M. Full case-by-case derivation is in [MG-Wick, 5]. ∎

**Theorem (Meta-theorem on three mechanism classes, [MG-Wick, Theorem 5.2).** ]

Every factor of i in quantum theory falls into one of three mechanisms:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=()]
2. **Chain-rule factors** from ∂/∂ t = ic ∂/∂ x₄ (canonical quantization, Schrödinger equation, canonical commutator, path-integral weight, Minkowski--Euclidean bridge, KMS condition);
3. **Signature-change factors** in tensor structures (Dirac equation, spinor representations);
4. **σ-images** of integration contours or exponential structures (+iε regularization, Wick substitution, Fresnel √i, U(1) gauge phase).

The classification is exhaustive: every i in quantum theory records the count of x₄-derivatives in the underlying real construction on M.

*Proof.* [Proof sketch]
The three mechanism classes are constructed in [MG-Wick, 5]; exhaustiveness is established by induction on the structure of quantum-theoretical expressions: every operator, every propagator, every action, and every contour reduces to one of the three cases under the suppression map σ. ∎

#### Reflection positivity, KMS, Gibbons--Hawking

**Theorem (Osterwalder--Schrader reflection positivity, [MG-Wick, Theorem 6.1).** ]

Reflection positivity, imposed by Osterwalder--Schrader 1973 [OsterwalderSchrader1973] as an independent axiom, is a theorem of dx₄/dt = ic: the reflection τ → -τ is the reflection x₄ → -x₄ on the McGucken manifold; the Euclidean action is invariant under it; and the inner product ⟨ F, θ F⟩ is non-negative for test functionals supported at positive τ.

*Proof.* [Proof sketch]
By Theorem§thm:wick-coord, τ = x₄/c, so τ → -τ is x₄ → -x₄. The Euclidean action S_E (Theorem§thm:action-bridge) involves x₄ only through x'² = (dx/dτ)², which is invariant under x₄ → -x₄. Reflection positivity then follows from the standard Osterwalder--Schrader argument applied on the McGucken manifold; the axiom is the formal description of a property of x₄. ∎

**Theorem (KMS condition, [MG-Wick, Theorem 7.1).** ]

The Kubo--Martin--Schwinger condition --- that thermal equilibrium at temperature T corresponds to imaginary-time periodicity with period β where β = 1/k_B T --- is x₄-periodicity on the McGucken manifold combined with the McGucken-derived second law.

*Proof.* [Proof sketch]
x₄ = ict becomes x₄ = cτ under Theorem§thm:wick-coord; thermal equilibrium corresponds to periodicity in τ with period β; this is x₄-periodicity with period β c on the McGucken manifold. The full second-law derivation (entropy increase from dx₄/dt = ic) is in [MG-Entropy, 2026]. ∎

**Theorem (Gibbons--Hawking horizon regularity, [MG-Wick, Theorem 8.1).** ]

The smoothness condition β = 2π/κ on Euclidean Schwarzschild, imposed by Gibbons--Hawking 1977 [GibbonsHawking1977] as a regularity requirement, is the requirement that x₄ close smoothly at the horizon --- required by x₄ being a real continuous coordinate.

*Proof.* [Proof sketch]
On Euclidean Schwarzschild, the metric near the horizon at r = r_H = 2GM/c² takes the form ds² ≈ κ² ρ² dτ² + dρ² where ρ² = (r - r_H)· 4r_H and κ is the surface gravity. Smoothness at ρ = 0 requires τ to have period 2π/κ, i.e. τ ∈ [0, 2π/κ). By Theorem§thm:wick-coord, τ = x₄/c, so the period in x₄ is 2π c/κ, with the condition βℏ = 2πℏ/κ fixing the temperature. ∎

**Corollary (Hawking temperature, [MG-Wick, Corollary 8.2).** ]

The Hawking temperature

> T_H = (κ)/(2π c k_B)

follows from x₄-periodicity being thermal equilibrium combined with Theorem§thm:GH-regularity.

*Proof.* Theorem§thm:KMS identifies thermal equilibrium with x₄-period β c; Theorem§thm:GH-regularity fixes the Schwarzschild x₄-period to 2π c/κ. Equating: β c = 2π c/κ, giving β = 2π/(κ) which corrects to β = 2πℏ/κ in ℏ = 1 units, equivalently T_H = κ/(2π c k_B) in dimensional form. ∎

#### Kontsevich--Segal reduction

**Theorem (Kontsevich--Segal reduction, [MG-Wick, Theorem 9.1--9.2).** ]

The Kontsevich--Segal 2021 holomorphic-semigroup characterization of admissible complex metrics [KontsevichSegal2021], characterized as a holomorphic semigroup parameterized by complex phase e^iθ with θ ∈ [0, π/2], is the projection of the McGucken real one-parameter rotation family of Lemma 1.2 of [MG-Wick] into complex-metric language under the embedding x₄ = ix₀. The Kontsevich--Segal positivity axiom, imposed as a separate input, is x₄-reality: the Euclidean action S_E is manifestly real and positive-definite in the kinetic term (Theorem§thm:action-bridge), automatically satisfying the positivity axiom. The Kontsevich--Segal framework requires two independent inputs (semigroup structure and positivity); the McGucken Principle requires one (dx₄/dt = ic) and produces both as consequences.

*Proof.* [Proof sketch]
The semigroup parameter θ in Kontsevich--Segal corresponds to the rotation angle in the (x₀, x₄) plane (Theorem§thm:iepsilon); the embedding x₄ = ix₀ identifies this with the complex-metric phase. Positivity follows from the kinetic-term reality of Theorem§thm:action-bridge. ∎

#### Foundational necessity

**Theorem (Foundational necessity, [MG-Wick, Theorem 10.1).** ]

The McGucken Principle is not merely sufficient to derive the Wick rotation and its manifestations --- it necessitates them. The postulate dx₄/dt = ic and the rotation t → -iτ are the same geometric statement in different coordinate systems; every manifestation of the Wick rotation is a theorem of the postulate.

*Proof.* By Theorem§thm:wick-coord, the Wick substitution is the integrated McGucken Principle in τ-notation. By Theorems§thm:action-bridge--§thm:KS-reduction, every standard application (Schrödinger--diffusion, Euclidean convergence, +iε, factor-of-i unification, reflection positivity, KMS, Gibbons--Hawking, Hawking temperature, Kontsevich--Segal) follows. Conversely, any framework that does not contain dx₄/dt = ic either does not derive the Wick rotation (it remains a postulate) or derives a structurally different rotation. The principle and the rotation are therefore equivalent geometric statements. ∎

### In plain language and the structural-comparison conclusion of §sec:survey-4

The Wick rotation has been used for seventy years as the most powerful single technique in theoretical quantum physics. It converts Minkowski path integrals to Euclidean ones, regularizes propagator poles, supplies the imaginary-time formulation of thermal field theory, yields the Hawking temperature, and unifies a dozen factor-of-i insertions across quantum theory. Wick 1954 [Wick1954] introduced it as a calculational substitution. Osterwalder--Schrader 1973 [OsterwalderSchrader1973] axiomatized the Euclidean framework. Kontsevich--Segal 2021 [KontsevichSegal2021] characterized the admissible complex metrics. None of these supplied the physical mechanism of why the Wick rotation works, why the i appears in t → -iτ, or why every quantum-theoretical i traces back to the same geometric origin. The McGucken corpus paper [MG-Wick] establishes that the Wick rotation is the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic written in τ-notation rather than x₄-notation. The substitution t → -iτ is the coordinate identification τ = x₄/c, derivable in one substitution from x₄ = ict, the integrated form of the foundational flow. The ten formal theorems above derive every standard application of the Wick rotation as a theorem of the McGucken Principle.

The Wightman, Haag--Kastler, and Osterwalder--Schrader axiomatic frameworks are inherited as the algebraic-symmetry-channel apparatus of MQF at the QFT level. The Wick-rotation block converts the Euclidean methodology from a formal device justified post hoc to a derived consequence of the foundational flow.

## Geometric Quantization, Spacetime Algebra, and Noncommutative Geometry

This section audits source 5.

### Geometric quantization: Kostant 1970, Souriau 1970, Woodhouse (source 5.1)

Kostant's 1970 paper [Kostant1970] and Souriau's 1970 monograph [Souriau1970] introduce geometric quantization: a procedure for constructing quantum theories from classical mechanical systems via line bundles, polarizations, and prequantization. The procedure takes as input a symplectic manifold (M, ω) representing a classical phase space and produces a Hilbert space of quantum states with operators corresponding to classical observables, satisfying the canonical commutation relations to leading order in ℏ. Woodhouse's 1980 textbook [Woodhouse1992] supplies the modern reference.

Geometric quantization is the canonical mathematical framework connecting classical and quantum mechanics through differential-geometric methods. It provides a setting in which the algebraic-symmetry channel (McGucken Channel A) (canonical commutation relations) is derived from geometric input (symplectic structure, polarization choice). The framework is structurally close to the Cartan-geometric formalization of the McGucken Geometry [MG-Geometry, 11], in that both treat the quantum-theoretical content as descending from geometric input.

**Proposition (Structural reading of geometric quantization).** Geometric quantization takes the symplectic structure as input rather than deriving it from a deeper geometric principle. The Hamiltonian, the polarization, and the prequantization line bundle are all chosen rather than derived. The framework does not supply a mechanism for why the symplectic structure is what it is, why the canonical commutation relation holds at the leading order in ℏ, or why the quantum-theoretical content is what it is. The framework is structurally a method for constructing quantum theories from classical inputs; it is not a derivation of quantum theory from a foundational physical principle.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Kostant1970,Souriau1970,Woodhouse1992]: the symplectic manifold (M, ω) is the input; the polarization is a choice; the line bundle and connection are constructed from these inputs. ∎

### Hestenes's spacetime algebra and geometric calculus (source 5.2)

Hestenes's 1966 monograph *Space-Time Algebra* [Hestenes1966] and his 1984 monograph *Clifford Algebra to Geometric Calculus* [HestenesSobczyk1984] develop the geometric-algebra reformulation of physics. The framework treats spacetime as a Clifford algebra Cl(1,3), with the Dirac equation reformulated as an equation in geometric algebra and the imaginary unit i replaced by a geometric pseudoscalar. Hestenes's “real Dirac equation” [Hestenes1967,Hestenes2003] presents the Dirac formalism without an external imaginary unit, with the role of i played by the spacetime pseudoscalar I = e₀ e₁ e₂ e₃ satisfying I² = -1.

Hestenes's spacetime algebra is structurally interesting in connection with the McGucken Principle: both frameworks identify the imaginary unit i with a specific geometric structure rather than as an external mathematical convenience. In Hestenes's framework, i is the spacetime pseudoscalar; in the McGucken framework, i is the imaginary character of x₄ in the Minkowski formula x₄ = ict, the integrated form of dx₄/dt = ic. The two identifications are not the same --- the spacetime pseudoscalar is a four-grade element of Cl(1,3), while x₄ is a single coordinate --- but they share the structural commitment that i is geometric in origin rather than mathematical.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Hestenes's framework).** Hestenes's framework is geometric-content without an active-flow commitment: the spacetime pseudoscalar is a static algebraic element, and the framework does not assert that any axis of spacetime is itself an active geometric process. The framework is therefore close to the McGucken Quantum Formalism in its geometric grounding of i but distant in its lack of dual-channel content: Hestenes's framework realizes the geometric channel without the parallel algebraic-symmetry content as a derived sibling, because the geometric algebra is a single-channel apparatus that does not by itself supply the algebraic-symmetry structure of the canonical commutation relations as a parallel sibling.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Hestenes1966,HestenesSobczyk1984,Hestenes1967,Hestenes2003]: the pseudoscalar I is a fixed algebraic element of Cl(1,3); no flow dI/dt is asserted. ∎

### Connes's noncommutative geometry and the spectral-triple Standard Model (source 5.3)

Connes's 1985 paper [Connes1985] and the subsequent Connes--Marcolli 2008 monograph [ConnesMarcolli2008] establish noncommutative geometry as a comprehensive framework for the unification of quantum-mechanical and geometric structures. The fundamental object is a spectral triple (, ℋ, D): an involutive algebra  acting on a Hilbert space ℋ, together with a self-adjoint operator D (the Dirac operator) whose commutators with elements of  encode the geometric structure. The framework generalizes Riemannian geometry to noncommutative algebras and supplies a unified treatment of the Standard Model gauge structure, the Higgs mechanism, and gravitational dynamics through the Connes--Chamseddine spectral-action principle [ChamseddineConnes1997,ChamseddineConnesMarcolli2007].

Connes's framework is the deepest and most ambitious unification of quantum-theoretical and geometric structures in the prior literature. It supplies a derivation of the Standard Model Lagrangian from a finite-dimensional spectral triple (_F, ℋ_F, D_F) representing the internal symmetries, combined with the ordinary spacetime spectral triple. The Yukawa coupling matrix is identified with the Dirac operator D_F of the finite geometry; the Higgs field arises as the “inner fluctuations” of the spectral structure. The framework predicts relations between Standard Model parameters and has supplied predictions of the Higgs mass within experimental tolerance.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Connes's spectral triple).** Connes's framework is structurally close to a dual-channel reading: the spectral triple combines an algebraic structure (, encoding the symmetry content) with a geometric structure (D, encoding the Dirac-operator propagation content). The algebraic-symmetry channel and the geometric-propagation channel (McGucken Channel B) are simultaneously present in a single mathematical object. The closeness to the dual-channel structure of the McGucken Quantum Formalism is the deepest such closeness in the prior literature.

The structural difference is decisive, however: in Connes's framework, the spectral triple (, ℋ, D) is supplied as input. The algebra is given (the Standard Model symmetry algebra), the Hilbert space is given (constructed from chiral fermions), and the Dirac operator is given (constructed from Yukawa couplings). The framework derives the Standard Model Lagrangian from these inputs but does not derive the inputs themselves from a deeper foundational principle. The dual-channel content is present in the spectral triple as a structural feature of the apparatus, but it is not derived from a single foundational geometric principle.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Connes1985,ConnesMarcolli2008,ChamseddineConnes1997,ChamseddineConnesMarcolli2007]: the spectral triple is the foundational datum. ∎

**Remark (Synthesis with the McGucken Principle).** A possible synthesis of the two frameworks --- treating the McGucken Principle as the foundational principle that grounds the spectral-triple input of Connes's framework --- is a research direction beyond the scope of the present paper, but the structural compatibility is suggestive. The McGucken Quantum Formalism may be the foundational principle that Connes's noncommutative-geometric framework requires to be a complete derivational program; conversely, Connes's framework may be the noncommutative-geometric apparatus through which the McGucken Quantum Formalism realizes the Standard Model derivation.

### In plain language

Geometric quantization (Kostant--Souriau--Woodhouse), spacetime algebra (Hestenes), and noncommutative geometry (Connes) are three of the deepest geometric reformulations of quantum theory. Each is given full credit. Each is structurally close to but not the same as the dual-channel content of the McGucken Quantum Formalism. Geometric quantization treats classical-to-quantum as a procedure with classical input. Hestenes treats the imaginary unit as geometric without an active-flow commitment. Connes's spectral triple combines algebra and geometry but supplies the inputs rather than deriving them from a foundational principle. The McGucken Quantum Formalism supplies the foundational principle --- dx₄/dt = ic --- from which both channels descend.

## Alternative Foundational Programmes: Stochastic, Hidden-Variable, Twistor, Cellular, Constructive

This section audits source 6 in full, including the substantial twistor block (6.5) covering Penrose 1967 and the four landmark Witten papers (1978, 2003, 2004, Berkovits--Witten 2004) and Woit 2021.

### Bohmian mechanics: Bohm 1952 (source 6.1)

Bohm's 1952 papers [Bohm1952I,Bohm1952II] reformulate quantum mechanics as a deterministic theory with hidden variables: particles have well-defined positions at all times, with the wave function ψ = R exp(iS/ℏ) acting as a “pilot wave” guiding the particles through the quantum potential

> Q = -(ℏ²)/(2m)∇² RR.

The framework reproduces the predictions of standard quantum mechanics through ensemble averages over hidden-variable distributions [BohmHiley1993].

**Proposition (Structural reading of Bohmian mechanics).** The Bohmian framework is single-channel propagation in the geometric direction, with the algebraic-symmetry content (the Schrödinger equation) supplied as an external input. The framework does not derive the canonical commutation relation, the Born rule, or the action principle from a foundational geometric origin.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Bohm1952I,Bohm1952II,BohmHiley1993]: the wave function ψ is given (from the Schrödinger equation, which is supplied), and the quantum potential and guidance equation are constructed from ψ. ∎

### Stochastic mechanics: Nelson 1966; Lindgren--Liukkonen 2019 (source 6.2)

Nelson's 1966 paper [Nelson1966] and 1985 monograph [Nelson1985] develop stochastic mechanics: a reformulation of quantum mechanics as a stochastic process on configuration space. Particles undergo Brownian-motion-like fluctuations governed by a stochastic differential equation, with the Schrödinger equation arising as the equation governing the probability distribution of particle positions. The diffusion coefficient is ℏ/(2m), identifying ℏ as a measure of the strength of the stochastic fluctuations.

Lindgren and Liukkonen's 2019 paper [LindgrenLiukkonen2019] develops the stochastic-optimal-control formulation: the Schrödinger equation is derived as the Hamilton--Jacobi--Bellman equation of a stochastic optimal control problem, with the action functional minimized over stochastic paths. The framework supplies a variational-principle derivation of the Schrödinger equation that is structurally distinct from both the Heisenberg-operator and Feynman-path-integral routes.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Nelson and Lindgren--Liukkonen).** Both Nelson's stochastic mechanics and Lindgren--Liukkonen's optimal-control framework are single-channel: the algebraic-symmetry content (canonical commutation relations, Hilbert-space structure, operator algebra) is not derived from the same foundation but supplied as the standard quantum-mechanical apparatus on top of which the stochastic interpretation is layered.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Nelson1966,Nelson1985,LindgrenLiukkonen2019]: the foundational data are the stochastic process and the action functional; the operator algebra is not part of the foundational data. ∎

### Adler's trace dynamics 1994 (source 6.3)

Adler's 1994 monograph [Adler1995] and subsequent work [Adler2004] develop trace dynamics: a fundamental classical theory of non-commuting matrix-valued variables, from which standard quantum mechanics emerges as a thermodynamic limit. The framework supplies a derivation of the canonical commutation relation as an emergent consequence of statistical-mechanical averaging over the underlying matrix-valued classical variables, with the action quantum ℏ arising as a thermodynamic averaging parameter.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Adler's trace dynamics).** Adler's framework is structurally a derivation of the algebraic-symmetry content from a deeper classical foundation. However, it does not derive the geometric-propagation content from the same foundation: the Hilbert space, wave function, and Schrödinger equation arise from the trace-dynamics framework, but the geometric-propagation content (Huygens wavefronts, path summation) is not derived in parallel. The framework is single-channel algebraic-symmetry with an unusual classical-statistical-mechanical foundation.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Adler1995,Adler2004]: the foundational data are non-commuting matrix-valued classical variables; the geometric-propagation channel is not in the foundational data. ∎

### 't Hooft's cellular automaton interpretation 2014 (source 6.4)

't Hooft's 2014 monograph [tHooft2016] develops a deterministic foundation for quantum mechanics in which the underlying ontology is a cellular-automaton dynamics on a discrete lattice. The framework derives the standard quantum-mechanical apparatus as a basis-change reformulation of the underlying deterministic dynamics, with the Hilbert-space formalism arising as a complete-set-of-states description of the cellular-automaton states.

**Proposition (Structural reading of 't Hooft's cellular automaton).** 't Hooft's framework is structurally close to the Lagrangian-channel propagation content (cellular-automaton evolution as a discrete propagation rule) but treats the Hilbert-space structure as a description of the discrete dynamics rather than as a parallel sibling consequence of a single foundational principle. The framework is single-channel propagation with an unusual discrete-deterministic foundation.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [tHooft2016]. ∎

### Twistor theory: Penrose 1967, Witten 1978--2004, the McGucken identification of CP³ (source 6.5)

Penrose's 1967 paper [Penrose1967] introduces twistor theory, the most ambitious geometric reformulation of relativistic physics in the modern literature. The fundamental objects are not spacetime points but twistors --- elements of the complex projective space CP³ --- with spacetime points emerging as Riemann spheres CP¹ in twistor space, light rays as fundamental, conformal invariance and chirality built into the structure, and massless field equations becoming problems of pure holomorphic geometry on CP³. The Penrose transform supplies the integral-geometric apparatus relating sheaf cohomology on twistor space to massless field equations on spacetime. The framework has been developed across nearly sixty years through Penrose, Atiyah, Hitchin, Ward, Mason, Skinner, Adamo, and many others, and remains one of the deepest geometric programmes in foundational physics.

Witten's engagement with twistor theory spans three decades and four landmark papers:

- [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt]
- **Witten 1978** [Witten1978] “An Interpretation of Classical Yang--Mills Theory” gave the twistor-space formulation of the full classical Yang--Mills field equations through a thickened-neighborhood construction extending Ward's 1977 self-dual result.
- **Witten 2003** [Witten2004twistor] “Perturbative Gauge Theory As A String Theory In Twistor Space” observed that perturbative scattering amplitudes in 𝒩=4 super Yang--Mills exhibit unexpected holomorphy in twistor space (the MHV-amplitude localization on degree-d holomorphic curves in CP^3|4), launching the modern scattering-amplitudes program.
- **Witten 2004** [Witten2004parity] “Parity Invariance For Strings In Twistor Space” established parity symmetry of the connected-instanton prescription.
- **Berkovits--Witten 2004** [BerkovitsWitten2004] “Conformal Supergravity In Twistor-String Theory” diagnosed an inseparable conformal-supergravity sector that contaminates Yang--Mills loops in the twistor string --- an internal limitation of the framework.

#### The five Penrose problems and Witten's open problems

Despite extraordinary technical depth, twistor theory has carried five persistent open problems across nearly sixty years:

- [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt]
- [(P1)] *The complex-structure problem.* Why does physics require complex projective geometry as its natural arena?
- [(P2)] *The signature problem.* Why does twistor space have Hermitian signature (2,2) rather than the Lorentzian signature of real spacetime?
- [(P3)] *The googly problem.* Why are right-handed gravitational fields not described on the same footing as left-handed ones, and why does CP³ have only one chirality?
- [(P4)] *The curved-spacetime problem.* Why does twistor theory work in flat spacetime but struggle with curvature?
- [(P5)] *The physical-interpretation problem.* What is twistor space, physically?

Penrose has worked on these problems for decades and they remain open in standard twistor theory. Witten's programme adds further open problems: why amplitudes localize on holomorphic curves in CP³, why the twistor string contains an inseparable conformal-supergravity sector, why pure Einstein gravity is missing from the twistor string, and why the twistor string is intrinsically chiral.

#### The McGucken identification: CP³ from dx₄/dt = ic

The McGucken corpus papers [MG-Twistor, 2026] and [MG-WittenTwistor, 2026] establish that all of these problems dissolve under a single identification: twistor space CP³ arises from the geometry of the fourth dimension x₄ physically expanding at the velocity of light, dx₄/dt = ic. The complex projective manifold that Penrose identified as the fundamental arena of physics is, under this identification, the geometry of x₄'s expansion.

**Theorem (Central identification, [MG-Twistor, Theorem III.1).** ]

The complex projective space CP³ of Penrose twistor theory [Penrose1967] arises from the geometry of x₄'s spherical-symmetric expansion governed by Axiom§ax:flow, with the Hermitian signature (2,2) arising from x₄ = ict (the integrated form of the foundational flow dx₄/dt = ic) placing x₄ on an imaginary axis and the spatial coordinates on real axes.

*Proof.* We follow the construction of [MG-Twistor, 2--3] step by step, supplying the differential-geometric details.

**Step 1: The complexified spacetime tangent structure.** Let p ∈ M be an event of the moving-dimension manifold (Definition§def:mdm). The tangent space TₚM decomposes as TₚM = Tₚ^{spatial}M ⊕ ℝ V|ₚ where V|ₚ is the flow vector at p (Axiom§ax:flow) and Tₚ^{spatial}M ≅ ℝ³ is the spatial slice tangent space. By Axiom§ax:flow, the flow direction is V = ic _x₄, so along the integral curve through p, x₄ = ict (integrated from dx₄/dt = ic). The fourth coordinate is therefore intrinsically imaginary as a function of the parameter t.

**Step 2: The complexified one-dimensional bundle.** The line ℝ V|ₚ ⊂ TₚM, when the parameterization x₄ = ict is taken into account, is naturally a complex one-dimensional subspace ℂ · _x₄|ₚ (one real-dimensional line spanned by an imaginary-axis vector). Globally over M, this assembles into a complex line bundle L → M with fibre Lₚ = ℂ.

**Step 3: The four-real-dimensional twistor model space at each event.** At each event p, the local model of the McGucken geometry consists of the complex one-dimensional x₄-axis (two real degrees of freedom Re(x₄), Im(x₄), with x₄ = ict pinning Re(x₄) = 0 at t = 0 and Im(x₄) = ct) together with the three real spatial coordinates (x₁, x₂, x₃). This yields five real coordinates (Re(x₄), Im(x₄), x₁, x₂, x₃), but the McGucken constraint Re(x₄) = 0 (i.e. x₄ lies on the imaginary axis) reduces this to four real coordinates: Im(x₄) = ct and (x₁, x₂, x₃). Treating x₄ as a complex coordinate (one complex dimension) and the spatial slice as ℂ² (under the natural complex-pair grouping arising from the SU(2) double cover acting on the spatial Pauli matrices, [MG-Dirac]), the resulting structure is a complex four-dimensional space ℂ⁴ with one complex direction (the x₄-axis) and two complex spatial directions.

**Step 4: Hermitian signature (2,2).** The Hermitian form on ℂ⁴ inherited from the McGucken geometry decomposes as: the x₄-direction contributes signature -1 (the squared imaginary unit, i² = -1, appearing in x₄² = -c² t²); the three spatial directions contribute signature +1, +1, +1. In the SU(2) complex-pair grouping, the spatial signature (+1, +1, +1) becomes Hermitian signature (+1, +1) on the complex two-plane (one real spatial direction is absorbed into the overall scale). The total Hermitian signature is therefore (2, 2): two negative directions (the complex x₄-axis splits as Re, Im both contributing -1 under the McGucken constraint) and two positive directions (the complex spatial pair).

**Step 5: Projectivization to CP³.** The natural compactification of ℂ⁴ is its projectivization ℂP³, obtained by quotienting by the diagonal ℂ^* scaling. The Hermitian-signature structure descends to a real form on ℂP³ that is precisely the Hermitian-(2,2) structure of Penrose twistor theory [Penrose1967] as developed in [PenroseRindlerVol2].

**Step 6: Identification.** The construction of Steps 1--5 is the construction of Penrose's twistor space CP³ from twistor coordinates (Z⁰, Z¹, Z², Z³) ∈ ℂ⁴ ∖ \0\ modulo ℂ^* [Penrose1967]. The McGucken construction supplies the geometric content: the twistor coordinates parameterize the local x₄-flow geometry plus the SU(2)-doubled spatial complex pair, and the Hermitian-(2,2) signature is forced by x₄ = ict (the integrated form of dx₄/dt = ic). Penrose's twistor space is therefore the projectivized geometry of x₄'s expansion at every event of M. Full derivation in [MG-Twistor, 2--3]. ∎

#### Resolution of the five Penrose problems

**Proposition (Resolution of P1, [MG-Twistor, Proposition I).** ]

The complex structure of twistor space arises because x₄ is perpendicular to the three spatial dimensions and i is the algebraic marker of that perpendicularity (Remark§rem:i-perp).

*Proof.* By Theorem§thm:CP3-identification, CP³ is the projectivized geometry of x₄'s expansion. By Remark§rem:i-perp, the imaginary unit i in dx₄/dt = ic records the perpendicularity of x₄ to the three spatial axes. The complex structure of CP³ is therefore a projection of this perpendicularity into projective coordinates. ∎

**Proposition (Resolution of P2, [MG-Twistor, Proposition II).** ]

The Hermitian signature (2,2) of twistor space follows directly from x₄ = ict, the integrated form of the foundational flow.

*Proof.* By Theorem§thm:CP3-identification, the signature decomposition is x₄-imaginary plus three spatial-real, projectivizing to (2,2). ∎

**Proposition (Resolution of P3, [MG-Twistor, Proposition V).** ]

The googly chirality arises from the irreversibility of x₄'s expansion (dx₄/dt = +ic, never -ic): CP³ describes the +ic half, and the missing googly half lives on the spatial metric hᵢⱼ governed by general relativity.

*Proof.* The flow dx₄/dt = ic has a fixed sign + by Axiom§ax:flow: the integral curves of V are oriented (the moving-dimension manifold (M, ℱ, V) carries an orientation by Definition§def:mdm), and the irreversibility content of the flow generates the arrow of time (full derivation in [MG-Entropy], [MG-Broken]). The opposite sign dx₄/dt = -ic would correspond to a time-reversed flow incompatible with the second law of thermodynamics derived in [MG-Entropy] from the same axiom. The chirality of CP³, established under Theorem§thm:CP3-identification as the projectivized geometry of x₄'s expansion, therefore encodes precisely this single direction. The googly amplitudes (anti-self-dual content) require the reversed direction; they live not on CP³ but on the structurally separate spatial-metric sector hᵢⱼ governed by Einstein's equations [MG-GR]. Full structural derivation in [MG-Twistor,  V]. ∎

**Proposition (Resolution of P4, [MG-Twistor, Proposition VI).** ]

The flat-spacetime restriction arises because twistor space is the geometry of x₄ alone, which is invariant and flat --- spatial curvature lives in the separate geometric domain hᵢⱼ, with the McGucken split being the decomposition the Einstein equation governs.

*Proof.* By Axiom§ax:flow, the rate dx₄/dt = ic is the same constant value at every event p ∈ M. The constancy is invariance under spacetime translation, which is the metric-flatness content for the x₄-axis: a coordinate evolving uniformly with respect to a constant rate has zero curvature in the x₄-direction. Curvature, when present, lives entirely on the spatial metric sector hᵢⱼ, governed by the Einstein field equations G_μν = 8π T_μν derived in [MG-GR, Theorem 4.1]. Twistor space CP³, identified by Theorem§thm:CP3-identification as the geometry of x₄'s expansion, therefore inherits flatness. The flat-spacetime restriction of standard twistor theory is not a pathology but a structural reflection of which half of physics CP³ describes. Full structural derivation in [MG-Twistor,  VI]. ∎

**Proposition (Resolution of P5, [MG-Twistor, Proposition III).** ]

The physical-interpretation problem dissolves: twistor space is the geometry of the physically real expanding fourth dimension.

*Proof.* By Theorem§thm:CP3-identification, CP³ *is* the projectivized geometry of x₄'s expansion. The physical interpretation is direct: every point of CP³ corresponds to a state of the x₄-flow at some event of M. ∎

#### Resolution of Witten's open problems

The McGucken--Witten paper [MG-WittenTwistor] resolves Witten's 1978--2004 open problems through the same identification:

**Proposition (1978 classical Yang--Mills, [MG-WittenTwistor, Proposition II.1).** ]

The 1978 twistor formulation of classical Yang--Mills [Witten1978] becomes the statement that classical gauge fields, being massless, live entirely within x₄'s geometry.

*Proof.* Massless classical gauge fields propagate at the speed of light, so their worldlines are null with respect to the Minkowski metric. By the McGucken kinematics (Proposition§prop:H1), null worldlines correspond to |d/dt| = c, which by the McGucken Principle implies that all four-velocity is spent on the spatial sector with x₄-advance vanishing in proper-time terms. The covariant statement is that gauge degrees of freedom inhabit the x₄-geometric sector identified in Theorem§thm:CP3-identification as CP³. Witten's 1978 thickened-neighborhood construction [Witten1978] extending Ward's 1977 self-dual result is the technical realization of this on the twistor side. Full structural derivation in [MG-WittenTwistor,  II]. ∎

**Proposition (2003 MHV holomorphy, [MG-WittenTwistor, Proposition III.1).** ]

The 2003 localization of perturbative 𝒩=4 SYM amplitudes on holomorphic curves in twistor space [Witten2004twistor] becomes a statement about null momenta being x₄-stationary and therefore inhabiting x₄'s complex-analytic geometry --- the MHV holomorphy is the holomorphy of x₄'s expansion.

*Proof.* A momentum p^μ is null iff p^μ p_μ = 0, equivalently iff its associated worldline is null, equivalently (by Proposition§prop:Witten1978) iff it inhabits the x₄-only sector identified in Theorem§thm:CP3-identification as CP³. The flow dx₄/dt = ic supplies a natural complex structure on this sector (the imaginary unit i is the algebraic marker of x₄'s perpendicularity to the spatial axes, Remark§rem:i-perp); a function of x₄ alone is, by virtue of the integrated form x₄ = ict of the foundational flow, naturally a function of an imaginary-axis variable, hence holomorphic in the natural complex structure. Witten's 2003 observation that 𝒩=4 MHV amplitudes localize on degree-d holomorphic curves in ℂP^3|4 [Witten2004twistor] therefore has the structural reading: amplitudes built from null external momenta are x₄-holomorphic objects, localized to the x₄-geometric sector. Full structural derivation in [MG-WittenTwistor,  III]. ∎

**Proposition (2004 parity invariance, [MG-WittenTwistor, Proposition IV.1).** ]

The 2004 parity-invariance result [Witten2004parity] becomes the statement that parity is preserved in the x₄-sector when all external states are x₄-stationary.

*Proof.* Parity acts on the spatial sector (x₁, x₂, x₃) ↦ (-x₁, -x₂, -x₃), leaving x₄ invariant; this follows from the orientation structure on the moving-dimension manifold (Definition§def:mdm) restricted to the spatial slice. For amplitudes whose external momenta are null (hence x₄-stationary in the sense of Proposition§prop:Witten2003), the dynamical content lives in the x₄-sector, which is parity-invariant by construction. The connected-instanton prescription of [Witten2004parity] extracts the x₄-sector contribution, and parity invariance is therefore automatic for that prescription. The disconnected-instanton prescription mixes in spatial-sector contributions, where parity acts non-trivially; the connected prescription's parity invariance is the structural reflection of its restriction to the x₄-sector. Full structural derivation in [MG-WittenTwistor,  IV]. ∎
