**Proposition (Berkovits--Witten conformal supergravity contamination, [MG-WittenTwistor, Proposition V.1).** ]

The Berkovits--Witten conformal-supergravity contamination [BerkovitsWitten2004] becomes a diagnosable consequence of the twistor string conflating the self-dual half of gravity (which lives on x₄'s geometry) with the anti-self-dual half (which lives on hᵢⱼ), with the McGucken split supplying the clean separation.

*Proof.* Gravity in the McGucken framework decomposes into two structurally distinct halves: the self-dual half lives on the x₄-geometric sector (the same sector as classical massless gauge fields, Proposition§prop:Witten1978), and the anti-self-dual half lives on the spatial-metric sector hᵢⱼ governed by the Einstein field equations of [MG-GR, Theorem 4.1]. Berkovits--Witten's 2004 twistor string [BerkovitsWitten2004] constructs amplitudes by integration over twistor moduli; this integration captures the x₄-sector self-dual half but conflates it with conformal-supergravity contributions that are sourced by the missing hᵢⱼ half. The conformal-supergravity contamination is therefore the diagnostic signal that the construction has captured only one of the two McGucken halves. The clean Yang--Mills/gravity separation requires both halves to be accounted for separately, which the McGucken split supplies. Full structural derivation in [MG-WittenTwistor,  V]. ∎

**Proposition (Gravity-gap resolution, [MG-WittenTwistor, Proposition VI.1).** ]

The gravity gap Witten flagged in 2003 (no string theory whose instanton expansion reproduces general relativity) is structurally resolved: Einstein gravity is not missing but split across two geometric domains, with the twistor string capturing the x₄-half.

*Proof.* Witten 2003's observation [Witten2004twistor] that no twistor-string instanton expansion reproduces full general relativity has the McGucken reading: full general relativity comprises both the x₄-sector (self-dual half, captured by the twistor string) and the hᵢⱼ sector (anti-self-dual half, governed by Einstein equations on the spatial metric, [MG-GR]). The twistor string's instanton expansion captures only the x₄-sector half; the missing half is the hᵢⱼ half, which lives on a structurally different geometric domain (the spatial metric, not twistor space). The gap is therefore not a defect of the twistor string but an indicator of the McGucken split: the twistor string is the correct string-theoretic description of half of gravity, and the other half requires the spatial-metric description of [MG-GR]. Full structural derivation in [MG-WittenTwistor,  VI]. ∎

**Proposition (Chirality/googly resolution, [MG-WittenTwistor, Proposition VII.1).** ]

The chirality/googly problem [[Witten2004twistor,Witten2004parity,BerkovitsWitten2004] and Penrose 1967 [Penrose1967]] is resolved as the physical fact that x₄ expands in one direction only: dx₄/dt = +ic.

*Proof.* The googly problem in twistor theory is the apparent absence of anti-self-dual amplitudes in twistor-string constructions. Under the McGucken identification (Theorem§thm:CP3-identification), CP³ is the projectivized geometry of x₄'s expansion in the direction dx₄/dt = +ic. The opposite direction dx₄/dt = -ic is excluded by the irreversibility content of Axiom§ax:flow (the flow has a fixed sign; this is the geometric origin of the arrow of time, [MG-Entropy], [MG-Broken]). The chirality of CP³ encodes this single direction; anti-self-dual content lives on the structurally separate hᵢⱼ sector (Proposition§prop:gravity-gap). The googly half is therefore not absent from physics --- it inhabits hᵢⱼ rather than CP³ --- and the chirality of twistor space is the physical reflection of the irreversibility of x₄'s expansion. Full structural derivation in [MG-WittenTwistor,  VII]. ∎

#### Woit's Euclidean twistor unification

Woit's 2021 paper “Euclidean Twistor Unification” [Woit2021] develops a twistor-theoretic framework in which the Standard Model arises from Euclidean signature twistor geometry, with the Higgs field interpreted as a geometric pointer to spontaneous Lorentz symmetry breaking. The McGucken--Woit synthesis [MG-Woit, 2026] identifies structural connections between Woit's framework and the McGucken Principle through the geometric content of x₄ expansion: Woit's Higgs-as-geometric-pointer arises in the McGucken framework as the Higgs field marking the direction of x₄'s expansion, with Woit's Euclidean signature being the natural McGucken signature in which x₄ is treated as a real fourth coordinate.

#### Structural reading of standard twistor theory vs. MQF

**Proposition (Standard twistor theory is single-channel).** Standard twistor theory (Penrose 1967 plus Witten 1978--2004 plus Woit 2021 in their published forms) is single-channel geometric-propagation: it supplies a complex-geometric reformulation of physics, with field equations becoming holomorphic problems on CP³. The algebraic-symmetry channel content (canonical commutation relations, operator algebras, Stone--von Neumann uniqueness) is not derived in parallel from the twistor-geometric foundation.

*Proof.* Direct examination of the cited works. ∎

**Remark (Twistor space as the Lagrangian arena of MQF).** Twistor space, in the McGucken reading, is the Lagrangian-channel geometric arena; it is half of the McGucken framework. The McGucken Principle supplies the foundation that makes twistor theory dual-channel by adding the Hamiltonian channel as a parallel sibling.

#### In plain language (verbatim from source 6.5)

> Penrose's twistor theory is the most ambitious geometric reformulation of physics in the modern literature: spacetime points become Riemann spheres in CP³, light rays become fundamental, massless field equations become holomorphic problems. Witten's 1978--2004 papers extended the program to gauge-theory amplitudes and twistor strings. The framework is given full credit. What it does not contain is the physical mechanism of why CP³ is the natural arena of physics. The McGucken corpus papers [MG-Twistor] and [MG-WittenTwistor] establish that CP³ arises from the geometry of the fourth dimension x₄ physically expanding at the velocity of light. The five open problems of Penrose twistor theory and the open problems of Witten's twistor program all dissolve under this identification. The complex structure is x₄'s perpendicularity. The (2,2) signature is x₄ = ict. The googly chirality is the irreversibility of x₄'s expansion. The flat-spacetime restriction is x₄'s flatness, with curvature living on the separate spatial-metric domain. The MHV-amplitude holomorphy is the holomorphy of x₄'s expansion. Twistor space is, under the McGucken Principle, the geometric-propagation arena of dual-channel quantum theory.

### Schuller's constructive gravity 2020 (source 6.6)

Schuller's 2020 work on constructive gravity [Schuller2020] develops a framework in which the spacetime metric is constrained by the requirement of supporting predictive matter-field dynamics through a system of Schuller closure conditions. The framework supplies a constructive derivation of general relativity from matter-field dynamics consistent with closed causal structure.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Schuller constructive gravity).** Schuller's framework is structurally a derivation of the metric content from matter-field consistency. It is single-channel in the geometric direction (constructing the metric from matter dynamics) and does not realize the dual-channel content of the McGucken Principle, though it is structurally compatible with the McGucken Lagrangian's Einstein--Hilbert sector through the Schuller closure plus Lovelock's 1971 theorem (per [MG-Lagrangian, Proposition VI.3]).

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Schuller2020]. ∎

### In plain language

Bohmian mechanics, Nelson's stochastic mechanics, Lindgren--Liukkonen, Adler's trace dynamics, 't Hooft's cellular automata, Penrose--Witten--Woit twistor frameworks, and Schuller's constructive gravity are major alternative foundational programmes for quantum theory. Each is given full credit. Each is single-channel: each realizes one of the two channels (algebraic-symmetry or geometric-propagation) and treats the other as either input or as not addressed. None of them realizes the dual-channel commitment of the McGucken Principle --- the simultaneous derivation of both channels from a single foundational principle.

## Categorical QFT, Renormalization Group, Representation Theory, and Yang--Mills

This section audits source 7 in full, including 7.5's formal-distinction propositions reproduced as Propositions§prop:irred-alg, §prop:irred-prop, §prop:irred-cat, §prop:cat-novel below.

### Atiyah--Segal topological QFT and Lurie's cobordism hypothesis (source 7.1)

Atiyah's 1988 paper [Atiyah1988] and Segal's 1988 paper [Segal1988] introduce the categorical formulation of topological quantum field theory: a TQFT is a symmetric monoidal functor from the cobordism category Cob(n) (whose objects are (n-1)-manifolds and whose morphisms are n-dimensional cobordisms) to the category Vect of vector spaces. Lurie's 2008 cobordism hypothesis [Lurie2008] extends this to extended TQFT through (∞, n)-categories of cobordisms with corners.

The categorical-QFT framework is the most modern formal foundation for QFT, supplying a unified setting for topological QFT, conformal QFT, and (in factorization-algebra form [CostelloGwilliam2017,FreedTeleman2014]) perturbative QFT. The framework treats QFT as an algebraic structure on a category of geometric objects, with the “quantum” content encoded in the functorial assignment of vector spaces to manifolds and morphisms to cobordisms.

**Proposition (Structural reading of categorical QFT).** The categorical-QFT framework is structurally close to the dual-channel content in that it explicitly combines algebraic structure (vector spaces, tensor products) with geometric structure (cobordism category, manifold morphisms). The framework is, in this sense, dual-channel by construction: every TQFT realizes both an algebraic side (the assigned vector spaces) and a geometric side (the cobordism manifolds) simultaneously.

The structural difference from the McGucken Quantum Formalism, however, is that the categorical-QFT framework treats the dual-channel content as a definitional feature of the framework, not as a derived consequence of a foundational principle. The cobordism category is supplied as input (the geometric structure), the vector-space assignment is supplied as input (the functor), and the consistency conditions are supplied as input (monoidal axioms). The framework supplies a setting in which dual-channel content can be expressed; it does not derive the dual-channel content from a single foundational principle.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Atiyah1988,Segal1988,Lurie2008]: the categorical data are the foundational data; no derivation from a deeper principle is provided. ∎

### Wilson renormalization group and Polchinski's equation (source 7.2)

Wilson's 1971 papers [Wilson1971] introduce the renormalization group as a flow on the space of effective field theories, with high-energy degrees of freedom progressively integrated out to produce effective theories at lower energies. Polchinski's 1984 paper [Polchinski1984] supplies the exact renormalization-group equation, the differential-equation form of the Wilson flow.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Wilsonian RG).** The Wilsonian RG framework is structurally agnostic about the foundational origin of QFT: it operates on QFTs as input, computing the flow under coarse-graining, with no derivation of the QFT structure from a deeper principle. The framework is methodological rather than foundational; it supplies a way of organizing QFT calculations rather than a foundation for the QFT itself.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Wilson1971,Polchinski1984]: the input to the RG flow is a QFT; the output is its scale-dependent counterparts. ∎

The McGucken Lagrangian's Wilsonian-RG analysis [MG-Lagrangian, Theorem 4.1] establishes that the McGucken Lagrangian is dimensionally complete in the Wilsonian sense: all renormalizable mass-dimension-≤ 4 operators consistent with the symmetries are included. The Wilsonian-RG framework is therefore structurally compatible with the McGucken framework as a methodological extension.

### Wigner classification and Coleman--Mandula theorem (source 7.3)

Wigner's 1939 paper [Wigner1939] supplies the classification of unitary irreducible representations of the Poincaré group: the irreducible representations are labeled by mass m ∈ ℝ_+ and spin s ∈ (1/2)ℤ_+, with each (m, s) corresponding to a possible elementary-particle species. The Wigner classification supplies the foundational structure for the particle content of relativistic QFT.

Coleman and Mandula's 1967 paper [ColemanMandula1967] establishes that, in a relativistic QFT with a unique vacuum and analytic S-matrix, the only allowed continuous symmetries are tensor products of the Poincaré group and an internal symmetry group acting trivially on spacetime; non-trivial mixing of internal and spacetime symmetries is forbidden. Weinberg's 1964--1995 reconstruction theorems [Weinberg1964,Weinberg1995] supply the structural derivation of relativistic QFT from Lorentz invariance plus cluster decomposition.

**Proposition (Structural reading of Wigner--Coleman--Mandula--Weinberg).** The Wigner--Coleman--Mandula--Weinberg apparatus is single-channel algebraic-symmetry: the framework derives the QFT structure from symmetry constraints on the Hilbert space and the S-matrix. The framework does not derive the symmetry constraints themselves from a deeper geometric principle --- the Poincaré group is taken as input, the cluster-decomposition principle is taken as input, and the analyticity of the S-matrix is taken as input.

*Proof.* Direct examination of [Wigner1939,ColemanMandula1967,Weinberg1964,Weinberg1995]. ∎

The McGucken Quantum Formalism contains the Wigner--Coleman--Mandula--Weinberg apparatus as the algebraic-symmetry channel (McGucken Channel A) of the framework, with the symmetry content derived from dx₄/dt = ic's invariance content [MG-Lagrangian, Theorem 2.1].

### Yang--Mills gauge theory and the principal-bundle apparatus (source 7.4)

Yang--Mills gauge theory [YangMills1954] is the canonical framework for non-abelian gauge fields. Yang--Mills theory is formulated on a principal G-bundle over spacetime, with a connection one-form A and curvature two-form F = dA + A ∧ A. The Yang--Mills Lagrangian is

> ℒ_{YM} = -14Tr(F ∧ ★ F),

and the Yang--Mills equations are d ★ F + A ∧ ★ F - ★ F ∧ A = 0.

The Yang--Mills framework is structurally close to the Cartan-geometric formalization of the McGucken Geometry: both are connection-on-principal-bundle formalizations. The McGucken Lagrangian's Yang--Mills sector [MG-Lagrangian, Proposition VI.2] derives the Yang--Mills form as the unique gauge-invariant Lorentz-scalar renormalizable Lagrangian on a principal G-bundle for any compact Lie group G, with the gauge group as empirical input. The Yang--Mills framework is, in the McGucken reading, the algebraic-symmetry channel content of the gauge sector, with the geometric-propagation content supplied by the McGucken Principle's structural commitments.

### Formal distinction: single-channel versus dual-channel quantum theory (source 7.5)

The comprehensive survey of §sec:survey-2--§sec:survey-7 establishes that no prior framework realizes the dual-channel content of the McGucken Principle. This subsection establishes a formal categorical distinction through three definitions, four propositions, and four full self-contained proofs.

#### Three categories of quantum-theoretical framework

**Definition (Single-channel algebraic-symmetry framework).** A *single-channel algebraic-symmetry quantum framework* is a quantum-theoretical framework F whose foundational data consist of:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=(A)]
2. a separable Hilbert space ℋ;
3. a *-algebra  ⊆ ℬ(ℋ) closed under specified algebraic identities (e.g. canonical commutation relations);
4. a unitary representation of a symmetry group G on ℋ;

and in which any propagation-content (Huygens-wavefront propagation, path summation, action principle) is either supplied as an external input, derived as a consequence of (A1)--(A3), or not addressed.

A *morphism* of such frameworks F₁ → F₂ is a unitary U: ℋ₁ → ℋ₂ intertwining the algebraic and group-theoretic data.

**Examples:** Heisenberg matrix mechanics [Heisenberg1925], Stone--von Neumann [Stone1930,vonNeumann1931,vonNeumann1932], Wightman [Wightman1956,GardingWightman1964,StreaterWightman1964], Haag--Kastler [HaagKastler1964,Haag1996], Wigner classification [Wigner1939], Coleman--Mandula [ColemanMandula1967], Weinberg reconstruction [Weinberg1995], Adler trace dynamics [Adler1995,Adler2004].

**Definition (Single-channel geometric-propagation framework).** A *single-channel geometric-propagation quantum framework* is a quantum-theoretical framework F whose foundational data consist of:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=(P)]
2. a configuration manifold Σ (or spacetime M);
3. a propagation rule (path measure, stochastic process, pilot-wave guidance, cellular-automaton update, or analogous);
4. an action functional S (or its analog) on the path space;

and in which any algebraic-symmetry content (canonical commutation relations, operator algebras, Hilbert-space structure) is either supplied as an external input, derived as a consequence of (P1)--(P3), or not addressed.

**Examples:** Feynman path integral [Feynman1948], Dirac Lagrangian formulation [Dirac1933], Bohmian mechanics [Bohm1952I,Bohm1952II,BohmHiley1993], Nelson stochastic mechanics [Nelson1966,Nelson1985], Lindgren--Liukkonen [LindgrenLiukkonen2019], 't Hooft cellular automaton [tHooft2016], Hestenes spacetime algebra [Hestenes1966,HestenesSobczyk1984], Schuller constructive gravity [Schuller2020], standard twistor theory [Penrose1967,Witten1978,Witten2004twistor,Witten2004parity,BerkovitsWitten2004,Woit2021].

**Definition (Dual-channel quantum-theoretical framework).** A *dual-channel quantum-theoretical framework* is a quantum-theoretical framework F whose foundational data consist of a single principle P from which both:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=(D)]
2. an algebraic-symmetry channel (data of type (A1)--(A3) of Definition§def:Falg); and
3. a geometric-propagation channel (McGucken Channel B) (data of type (P1)--(P3) of Definition§def:Fprop);

descend as parallel sibling theorems through disjoint intermediate machinery (in the sense of Theorem§thm:disjoint below), with at least one fundamental quantum-theoretical identity (the canonical commutation relation, the Schrödinger equation, or analog) reached by both routes (the structural-overdetermination property, Lemma§lem:overdet).

**Remark (On exhaustiveness of these definitions).** The three definitions are not exhaustive of all conceivable quantum-theoretical foundations --- one can imagine constructor-theoretic frameworks (Deutsch--Marletto), categorical frameworks (Atiyah--Segal--Lurie), and others --- but they suffice for the categorical distinction at issue. Constructor theory and categorical QFT supply settings in which dual-channel content can be expressed but, as established in Propositions§prop:cat-QFT-input and §prop:connes-input, treat the dual-channel content as definitional or as input rather than as derived from a foundational principle.

#### The four irreducibility/novelty propositions (proofs in §sec:cat-novelty)

The formal-distinction propositions of source 7.5 --- Propositions 7.5.1--7.5.4 --- are reproduced and proven in §sec:cat-novelty below as Propositions§prop:irred-alg, §prop:irred-prop, §prop:irred-cat, and §prop:cat-novel, after the formal definition of the McGucken Quantum Formalism (§sec:hamiltonian--§sec:equivalence) provides the structural-overdetermination machinery that the proofs require.

#### Structural recap: what the four propositions establish (source 7.5.3)

The four propositions of this subsection establish, with formal proofs, that the McGucken Quantum Formalism is mathematically distinct from every category of quantum-theoretical framework prior literature has accommodated. The structural-mathematical content of this distinction can be summarized in three observations, reproduced verbatim from source 7.5.3.

**Observation 1: Different number of channels..** 
Single-channel algebraic-symmetry frameworks (Heisenberg, Wightman, Haag--Kastler, Wigner, Adler) realize one channel: algebraic-symmetry content, with propagation content treated as input or derived. Single-channel propagation frameworks (Feynman, Bohm, Nelson, Lindgren--Liukkonen, 't Hooft, Hestenes, Schuller) realize one channel: geometric-propagation content, with algebraic-symmetry content treated as input or derived. The McGucken Quantum Formalism realizes both channels as parallel sibling consequences of a single foundational principle. The number of foundationally derived channels is the categorical distinction.

**Observation 2: Structural overdetermination..** 
Single-channel frameworks supply one route to each fundamental quantum-theoretical identity: the canonical commutation relation, the Schrödinger equation, the Born rule, the action principle. The McGucken Quantum Formalism supplies two routes to the canonical commutation relation through disjoint intermediate machinery. The structural overdetermination is a categorical feature absent from all prior single-channel frameworks. Spectral-triple and categorical-QFT frameworks combine algebra and geometry but do not derive structural overdetermination from a foundational principle.

**Observation 3: Foundational depth..** 
Prior frameworks treat their foundational content (operator algebras, path integrals, spectral triples, cobordism functors) as inputs supplied at the categorical level. The McGucken Quantum Formalism derives its dual-channel content from a single foundational principle dx₄/dt = ic of geometric content. The framework therefore reaches a deeper structural level than the prior frameworks: it does not specify a category of objects, but supplies the foundational principle from which a categorical structure of dual-channel objects descends.

The conclusion of 7.5 is therefore stronger than the conclusion of 2--7.4 alone. The earlier sections established that no prior framework realizes the dual-channel content of the McGucken Principle. The present section establishes the structural-mathematical reason why no prior framework does so: the kind of quantum-theoretical content that the McGucken Principle articulates lives in a categorically different mathematical structure from the quantum-theoretical frameworks the prior literature has accommodated. Quantum theory could be operator-algebraic, path-integral, axiomatic-QFT, spectral-triple, or categorical-QFT --- each of these supplied a single channel or a setting for both channels --- but no framework, in standard formulation, derived both channels from a single foundational principle as parallel sibling theorems. The McGucken Quantum Formalism supplies the missing categorical structure.

### In plain language

Heisenberg matrix mechanics, the Stone--von Neumann theorem, Wightman axioms, Haag--Kastler algebraic QFT, Wigner classification --- all algebraic. Feynman path integral, Bohm pilot wave, Nelson stochastic, 't Hooft cellular, twistor theory --- all propagation. Connes spectral triple, Atiyah--Segal cobordism --- both at once but as input, not as derived. None of them derives both channels from a single principle. Four formal propositions in this section prove the difference: McGucken Quantum Formalism cannot be reduced to algebraic-symmetry frameworks (Proposition§prop:irred-alg), cannot be reduced to propagation frameworks (Proposition§prop:irred-prop), cannot be reduced to spectral-triple or categorical-QFT frameworks in their standard formulations (Proposition§prop:irred-cat), and as a category cannot be made equivalent to any of those by any structure-preserving functor (Proposition§prop:cat-novel). The dual-channel category is genuinely new. The mainstream tradition of quantum theory is rich; what it does not contain is a framework where both channels are parallel sibling consequences of a single foundational principle. The McGucken Quantum Formalism supplies what was missing.

Formal Definition of the McGucken Quantum Formalism

## The Dual-Channel Sextuple (Source 8--9)

The transition from survey to formalization (source 8) and the dual-channel sextuple definition (source 9) are fully reproduced in §sec:axioms above (Definitions§def:mdm, §def:qlayer, §def:MQF). The remainder of Part III formalizes the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian routes (McGucken Channels A and B, source 10--11) and proves the Equivalence Theorem (source 12).

## The Hamiltonian Route: Algebraic-Symmetry Channel (Source 10)

This section audits Propositions H.1--H.5 of the source paper. Each is restated, fully self-contained proofs are supplied, and every appearance of x₄ = ict is annotated as the integrated form of the foundational flow dx₄/dt = ic.

### Stone's theorem

**Theorem (Stone, 1930).** Let U(s)\_s ∈ ℝ be a strongly continuous one-parameter unitary group on a separable Hilbert space ℋ. Then there exists a unique self-adjoint operator A on ℋ (densely defined on the domain of strong differentiability of U) such that

> U(s) = e^-isA/ℏ    for all  s ∈ ℝ,

where A is the infinitesimal generator Aψ = iℏ ds|ₛ₌₀ U(s)ψ.

*Proof.* [Source]
Original: Stone 1930 [Stone1930]. Modern textbook proof: Reed--Simon, *Methods of Modern Mathematical Physics*, Vol. I (Academic Press, 1980), Theorem VIII.7--8; or Hall, *Quantum Theory for Mathematicians* (Springer, 2013), Theorem 10.15. ∎

### From the flow to the Minkowski metric

**Proposition (H.1: Minkowski metric forced by the flow).** Let (M, ℱ, V) be a moving-dimension manifold satisfying Axiom§ax:flow, and let (t, x₁, x₂, x₃) be local coordinates adapted to the foliation in a neighborhood of an event p ∈ M, with V = ₜ at p. Then the Lorentzian metric g at p takes the form

> ds² = -c² dt² + dx₁² + dx₂² + dx₃².

*Proof.* By Axiom§ax:flow, along the integral curve of V through p, the fourth-axis coordinate x₄ satisfies x₄ = ict (the integrated form of dx₄/dt = ic, Remark§rem:flow-primacy). The squared four-axis line element in coordinates where the four axes are mutually orthogonal Euclidean (orthogonality from Definition§def:mdm(iii), V transverse to ℱ) is

> ds² = dx₁² + dx₂² + dx₃² + dx₄².

Substituting dx₄ = ic dt and using i² = -1:

> dx₄² = (ic dt)² = i² c² dt² = -c² dt².

Therefore

> ds² = dx₁² + dx₂² + dx₃² - c² dt²,

the Minkowski line element of signature (-,+,+,+) at p. Smoothness of V extends this to a neighborhood. ∎

**Remark.** Proposition§prop:H1 uses the integrated form x₄ = ict, which is the coordinate consequence of the flow dx₄/dt = ic of Axiom§ax:flow. The Lorentzian signature is therefore a consequence of the geometric flow, not an independent postulate.

### Translation invariance forces a self-adjoint momentum generator

**Proposition (H.2: Momentum operator from spatial translation invariance).** Let the McGucken flow rate ic be invariant under spatial translations along the axis xⱼ (this invariance is a property of Axiom§ax:flow: the flow rate is the same at every event of M). Let Tⱼ(s)\_s ∈ ℝ be the unitary representation on ℋ of the corresponding one-parameter translation group acting along xⱼ. Then there exists a unique self-adjoint operator ⱼ on ℋ such that

> Tⱼ(s) = e^-isⱼ/ℏ.

*Proof.* The invariance of the flow rate ic under translation along xⱼ implies, by Wigner's theorem on quantum-mechanical symmetries [Wigner1939], that the symmetry is implemented by a strongly continuous one-parameter unitary group Tⱼ(s)\_s ∈ ℝ on ℋ. By Stone's theorem (Theorem§thm:Stone), there exists a unique self-adjoint operator ⱼ such that Tⱼ(s) = e^-isⱼ/ℏ. ∎

**Remark.** The factor ℏ in the exponent is, at this stage, a placeholder real constant fixing units. Its identification with Planck's reduced constant is established in [MG-Constants, 2026] from the McGucken-self-consistency principle.

### Configuration representation forces p

 = -iℏ ∂/∂ q

**Proposition (H.3: Differential form of the momentum operator).** In the configuration-space representation ℋ = L²(ℝ³, dq₁ dq₂ dq₃) in which ⱼ acts as multiplication by qⱼ, the momentum operator ⱼ of Proposition§prop:H2 acts on the dense domain 𝒮(ℝ³) (Schwartz space) by

> ⱼψ(q) = -iℏ (∂ ψ)/(∂ qⱼ)(q).

*Proof.* In the configuration representation, the unitary translation Tⱼ(s) acts by

> (Tⱼ(s)ψ)(q) = ψ(q₁, …, qⱼ + s, …, q₃).

For ψ ∈ 𝒮(ℝ³), the map s ↦ Tⱼ(s)ψ is strongly differentiable at s = 0 with derivative ∂ ψ/∂ qⱼ. By Stone's theorem (Theorem§thm:Stone), the generator of Tⱼ(s) = e^-isⱼ/ℏ satisfies

> ⱼψ = iℏ ds|ₛ₌₀Tⱼ(s)ψ.

Differentiating e^-isⱼ/ℏ at s = 0 gives -iⱼ/ℏ; equating -iⱼ/ℏ = ⱼ in the configuration representation yields

> ⱼ = -iℏ ⱼ.

The factor i here descends from the imaginary character of x₄ in x₄ = ict, the integrated form of the foundational flow dx₄/dt = ic. ∎

### Direct computation of the canonical commutator

**Proposition (H.4: Canonical commutation relation, Hamiltonian-route derivation).** With ⱼ acting as multiplication by qⱼ and ₖ = -iℏ ₖ on the dense common invariant domain 𝒮(ℝ³) ⊂ L²(ℝ³), one has

> [ⱼ, ₖ] = iℏ ⱼₖ 1.

*Proof.* For ψ ∈ 𝒮(ℝ³),

> (ⱼ ₖ ψ)(q) &= qⱼ (-iℏ ₖψ)(q) = -iℏ qⱼ ₖψ(q),
> (ₖ ⱼ ψ)(q) &= -iℏ ₖ(qⱼψ)(q) = -iℏ ⱼₖψ(q) - iℏ qⱼ ₖψ(q),

where the second equality uses the product rule. Subtracting:

> [ⱼ, ₖ]ψ(q) = -iℏ qⱼₖψ + iℏ ⱼₖψ + iℏ qⱼₖψ = iℏ ⱼₖ ψ(q).

Hence [ⱼ, ₖ] = iℏ ⱼₖ 1 on 𝒮(ℝ³), and by density extension on the closure in L². ∎

### Closure by Stone--von Neumann uniqueness

**Theorem (Stone--von Neumann).** Let U(s)\_s ∈ ℝⁿ and V(t)\_t ∈ ℝⁿ be strongly continuous unitary representations of ℝⁿ on a separable Hilbert space ℋ satisfying the Weyl form of the canonical commutation relations

> U(s)V(t) = e^is · t  V(t)U(s).

If the joint representation is irreducible, it is unitarily equivalent to the Schrödinger representation on L²(ℝⁿ, dq) in which U(s) = e^-is/ℏ, V(t) = e^-it/ℏ with  multiplication and  = -iℏ∂.

*Proof.* [Source]
[Stone1930,vonNeumann1931,vonNeumann1932]; modern exposition [Mackey1949,Rieffel1972,Rosenberg2004]. ∎

**Proposition (H.5: Uniqueness of the Hamiltonian-route representation).** The representation of the canonical commutation relations(eq:CCR) constructed in Propositions§prop:H2--§prop:H4 is unique up to unitary equivalence.

*Proof.* The operators ⱼ, ₖ on ℋ = L²(ℝ³) satisfy(eq:CCR); their joint action is irreducible (the only bounded operator on L²(ℝ³) commuting with all ⱼ and ₖ is a scalar multiple of the identity, by the Stone--von Neumann argument). By Theorem§thm:SvN, any other irreducible representation of(eq:CCR) on a separable Hilbert space is unitarily equivalent. ∎

### Summary of the Hamiltonian route (McGucken Channel A)

The chain

> {Axiom§ax:flow{H.1}Minkowski{H.2 (Stone)}ⱼ{H.3}-iℏⱼ{H.4}[,]=iℏ{H.5 (Stone--vN)}unique}

is self-contained. Every step uses either a property of the flow, a cited theorem, or direct computation.

## The Lagrangian Route: Geometric-Propagation Channel (Source 11)

This section audits Propositions L.1--L.6 of the source paper. The route uses entirely disjoint intermediate machinery from §sec:hamiltonian: Huygens-wavefront propagation, path-space discretization, accumulated x₄-phase, Gaussian short-time integration, and direct computation. Stone's theorem and the Stone--von Neumann theorem do not enter the Lagrangian route.

### Huygens' Principle from the spherical-symmetric flow

**Proposition (L.1: Huygens' Principle as a theorem).** Let (M, ℱ, V) be a moving-dimension manifold satisfying Axiom§ax:flow. For every event p ∈ M and every Δ t > 0, the spherical wavefront Wₚ(Δ t) centered at p has the property that each point q ∈ Wₚ(Δ t) is itself an event of M from which a sub-wavefront W_q(Δ t') emanates for every Δ t' > 0. The wavefront Wₚ(Δ t + Δ t') is the envelope (in the spatial slice at parameter Δ t + Δ t') of the family W_q(Δ t') : q ∈ Wₚ(Δ t)\.

*Proof.* By Axiom§ax:flow, every event of M is the center of a spherically symmetric expanding wavefront with rate c. Since Wₚ(Δ t) consists of events of M, each event q ∈ Wₚ(Δ t) has its own wavefront W_q of rate c. The set of points reached at parameter Δ t + Δ t' from p via the two-step process (first reach q ∈ Wₚ(Δ t), then reach a point of W_q(Δ t')) is _q ∈ Wₚ(Δ t) W_q(Δ t'). The boundary of this union, in the spatial slice at parameter Δ t + Δ t', is the envelope. By the constancy of c (Axiom§ax:flow), the envelope is the sphere of radius c(Δ t + Δ t') centered at p, which is Wₚ(Δ t + Δ t'). This is Huygens' Principle. ∎

**Remark.** The classical statement of Huygens' Principle (Huygens, *Traité de la Lumière*, 1690 [Huygens1690]) was an empirical principle of wave propagation. Proposition§prop:L1 establishes it as a theorem of Axiom§ax:flow.

### Path space generated by iterated wavefront expansions

**Proposition (L.2: Path-space generation).** Let x_A, x_B be two events in M with x_A at time t_A and x_B at time t_B > t_A. Discretize [t_A, t_B] into N equal sub-intervals of duration ε = (t_B - t_A)/N. Define 𝒫_N(x_A, x_B) as the set of piecewise paths (x₀ = x_A, x₁, …, x_N = x_B) with xₖ₊₁ ∈ W_xₖ(ε) for each k. Then _N 𝒫_N(x_A, x_B) is dense (in the topology of uniform convergence on compact sets) in the space 𝒞([t_A, t_B], M) of continuous paths from x_A at t_A to x_B at t_B.

*Proof.* Fix continuous γ: [t_A, t_B] → M. By uniform continuity on the compact interval, for any η > 0 there exists δ > 0 such that |t - t'| < δ ⇒ dist(γ(t), γ(t')) < η. Choose N with ε < δ. Define _N by sampling γ at tₖ = t_A + kε. By Axiom§ax:flow, the flow rate is c, so each _N(tₖ₊₁) lies on W__N(tₖ)(ε), hence _N ∈ 𝒫_N. As N → ∞, _N → γ uniformly. Density follows. ∎

### Accumulated x₄-phase along a path

**Proposition (L.3: x₄-phase equals classical action).** Along γ ∈ 𝒞([t_A, t_B], M), the accumulated x₄-phase is

> Φ[γ] = _γ (dx₄)/(dt) dt = _t_A^t_B ic d_γ,

where _γ is proper time along γ. In the non-relativistic limit with the McGucken Lagrangian density ℒ [MG-Lagrangian, 2026], the phase reduces to

> Φ[γ] = ℏ S[γ],      S[γ] = _t_A^t_B ℒ(x,  x, t) dt.

*Proof.* By Axiom§ax:flow, dx₄/dt = ic is constant along proper time. Hence

> Δ x₄ = _t_A^t_B (dx₄)/(dτ) dτ = ic τ,

where τ is total proper time along γ. The relativistic free-particle action is S_{free}[γ] = -mc² τ, so Δ x₄ = ic τ = -i S_{free}/(mc). In units with action quantum ℏ (set by [MG-Constants, 2026]), the phase factor associated with Δ x₄ is exp(Δ x₄ · mc/ℏ) = exp(iS_{free}/ℏ) after orientation conventions. The general extension follows by integrating the McGucken Lagrangian. ∎

**Remark.** The factor i in e^iS/ℏ traces directly to the imaginary unit in the foundational flow dx₄/dt = ic. The accumulated x₄-displacement is itself imaginary in integrated form; the phase factor records this. This is the geometric origin of the imaginary unit in the Feynman path integral.

### Sum over paths reproduces the Feynman path integral

**Proposition (L.4: Feynman path integral).** The propagator from (x_A, t_A) to (x_B, t_B) is

> K(x_B, t_B; x_A, t_A) = _𝒞([t_A,t_B],M) 𝒟γ  exp(ℏ S[γ]),

where 𝒟γ is the Wiener-type measure inherited from the discretized measures on 𝒫_N as N → ∞.

*Proof.* By Proposition§prop:L2, 𝒫_N is dense in 𝒞. By Axiom§ax:flow, each event of 𝒫_N is reached from its predecessor via wavefront expansion that distributes amplitude isotropically over W(ε). The discretized propagator is

> K_N = ∫ dx₁⋯ dx_N-1 ₖ₌₀^N-1 A(xₖ₊₁, xₖ; ε).

By Proposition§prop:L3, each single-step amplitude carries phase exp(iSₖ/ℏ) with Sₖ = _tₖ^tₖ₊₁ℒ dt. The product telescopes into exp(iS[γ]/ℏ), and N → ∞ yields the Feynman path integral. ∎

### Schrödinger equation as the short-time limit

**Proposition (L.5: Schrödinger equation from the path integral).** ψ(x, t)  ∫ dx_A  K(x, t; x_A, t_A) ₀(x_A) satisfies iℏ ₜψ = ψ with  = -(ℏ²/2m)∇² + V.

*Proof.* For the kinetic-plus-potential Lagrangian ℒ = 12m x² - V(x), the single-step amplitude over interval ε from x' to x is, up to normalization A(ε),

> K(x, x'; ε) = A(ε) exp[ℏ((m(x - x')²)/(2ε) - V(x) ε)].

Set y = x - x' and Taylor expand ψ(x', t) = ψ(x - y, t) = ψ - y ₓψ + 12y² ₓ²ψ + O(y³):

> ψ(x, t + ε) &= A(ε) e^-iε V/ℏ∫ dy e^imy²/2ε[ψ - y ₓψ + 12y² ₓ²ψ + ⋯].

Gaussian integrals: ∫ dy e^imy²/2ε = √2π iε/m, ∫ y e^imy²/2εdy = 0, ∫ y² e^imy²/2εdy = (iε/m)√2π iε/m. Choose A(ε) = √m/(2π iε):

> ψ(x, t+ε) = e^-iε V/ℏ[ψ + (iε)/(2m)ₓ²ψ + O(ε²)].

Expand both sides in ε:

> ε ₜψ = -(iε V)/(ℏ)ψ + (iε)/(2m)ₓ²ψ + O(ε²).

Divide by ε and multiply by iℏ:

> iℏ ₜψ = Vψ - (ℏ²)/(2m)ₓ²ψ = ψ.

The three-dimensional case is identical with ₓ² → ∇². This is Feynman's 1948 derivation [Feynman1948]. ∎

### Direct computation of the canonical commutator (Lagrangian route)

**Proposition (L.6: Canonical commutation relation, Lagrangian-route derivation).** From the Schrödinger equation derived in Proposition§prop:L5, the configuration-space wave function ψ(q, t) satisfies ψ = -iℏ _qψ in the standard reading of Schrödinger quantum mechanics ( generates translations on the wave function). Direct computation as in Proposition§prop:H4 gives

> [ⱼ, ₖ]ψ = iℏ ⱼₖ ψ.

*Proof.* Identical algebraic computation to Proposition§prop:H4; the route to ,  proceeds via Schrödinger-equation derivation from the path integral (Propositions§prop:L1--§prop:L5) rather than via Stone's theorem. ∎

### Disjointness of the intermediate machinery

**Theorem (Disjointness of routes).** The intermediate machinery used in the Hamiltonian route (Propositions§prop:H1--§prop:H5) is disjoint from the intermediate machinery used in the Lagrangian route (Propositions§prop:L1--§prop:L6). The two routes share only:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=()]
2. the foundational Axiom§ax:flow (the flow dx₄/dt = ic);
3. the Lorentzian metric structure (Proposition§prop:H1, used implicitly by the Lagrangian route to identify proper time);
4. the final canonical commutator [, ] = iℏ 1.

The Hamiltonian route uses Stone's theorem (Theorem§thm:Stone), the Stone--von Neumann uniqueness theorem (Theorem§thm:SvN), and the configuration representation. The Lagrangian route uses Huygens-wavefront propagation (Proposition§prop:L1), path-space density (Proposition§prop:L2), accumulated x₄-phase (Proposition§prop:L3), and Gaussian short-time integration (Proposition§prop:L5). No element of either route's exclusive machinery appears in the other.

*Proof.* Inspection of Propositions§prop:H1--§prop:H5: their proofs invoke Stone's theorem and Stone--von Neumann; no Huygens, no path-space, no Gaussian integration. Inspection of Propositions§prop:L1--§prop:L6: their proofs invoke Huygens, path-space density, and Gaussian integration; no Stone, no Stone--vN. Shared elements (i)--(iii) are explicitly identified. ∎

## The Equivalence Theorem and Structural Overdetermination (Source 12)

This section audits source 12.

### Equivalence of three presentations

**Theorem (Equivalence of three presentations of MQF).** Let (M, ℱ, V; ℋ, , ψ) be a McGucken Quantum Formalism in the sense of Definition§def:MQF. The following three presentations are mathematically equivalent:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=()]
2. the dual-channel sextuple (M, ℱ, V; ℋ, , ψ) (Definition§def:MQF);
3. the Heisenberg-group representation (ℋ, U(s), V(t)\, ) on ℋ satisfying the Weyl form of the canonical commutation relations and irreducibility (the Hamiltonian-route presentation, §sec:hamiltonian);
4. the Feynman path-integral presentation with action S derived from the McGucken Lagrangian, on the configuration manifold over M with paths summed via Wiener-type measure (the Lagrangian-route presentation, §sec:lagrangian).

The equivalence is via unitary maps preserving all algebraic and propagation structures.

*Proof.* **(I) ⇒ (II).** From the sextuple, extract ℋ and . The CCR-generators ,  are part of  (Q2). Their Weyl-form exponentials U(s) = e^-is/ℏ, V(t) = e^-it/ℏ form an irreducible representation by Q4 and Proposition§prop:H5. The Hamiltonian  is the time-translation generator (Q3, Proposition§prop:H2). This recovers (II).

**(II) ⇒ (III).** From the irreducible Heisenberg representation, the configuration representation L²(ℝ³, dq) exists by Stone--von Neumann (Theorem§thm:SvN). The Schrödinger equation iℏ ₜψ = ψ has unique strongly continuous solutions; the propagator K(x_B, t_B; x_A, t_A) = ⟨ x_B|e^-i(t_B-t_A)/ℏ|x_A⟩ admits the path-integral expression by Feynman's 1948 argument [Feynman1948] run in reverse (Proposition§prop:L4). The action is determined by  via Legendre transform of the underlying Lagrangian. This recovers (III).

**(III) ⇒ (I).** From the path integral with action S, the propagator yields the Schrödinger equation by Proposition§prop:L5; the propagator's matrix elements determine ℋ and ; the canonical commutator descends from the action via Legendre transform (Proposition§prop:L6). The geometric data (M, ℱ, V) are the configuration spacetime on which the path integral is defined. The dual-channel structural condition Q4 is the content of Theorem§thm:disjoint: both routes (II) and (III) descend from Axiom§ax:flow. ∎

### Structural Overdetermination Lemma

**Lemma (Structural Overdetermination, [MG-Foundations, Lemma 5.2).** ]

The canonical commutation relation [, ] = iℏ 1 is reached as a theorem of Axiom§ax:flow by both the Hamiltonian route (McGucken Channel A) (Proposition§prop:H4) and the Lagrangian route (McGucken Channel B) (Proposition§prop:L6), through disjoint intermediate machinery (Theorem§thm:disjoint). This is structural overdetermination: the same physical content is reached by two independent derivations.

Equivalently: if Axiom§ax:flow were modified to dx₄/dt = iα c for any α ≠ 1, both routes would produce [, ] = iα 1 after rescaling; if Axiom§ax:flow were modified to remove the imaginary unit, both routes would produce a real commutator and quantum mechanics would not exist; if Axiom§ax:flow were modified to remove the spherical-symmetry requirement, the Lagrangian route would fail (no Huygens) while the Hamiltonian route would survive --- and structural overdetermination would be lost. The structural-overdetermination property is therefore a sensitive diagnostic of the foundational principle: dx₄/dt = ic is not just a sufficient principle, it is the principle that yields both routes consistently.

*Proof.* The first paragraph is a direct restatement of Theorem§thm:disjoint combined with Propositions§prop:H4 and §prop:L6. The counterfactual modifications are immediate consequences of running each route with the modified axiom and observing where the derivations break. ∎

**Remark (Significance of structural overdetermination).** Structural overdetermination is the diagnostic that distinguishes a correct foundational principle from a sufficient one. A sufficient principle generates the desired result through one chain; a correct principle generates the desired result through multiple independent chains, with the multiplicity itself being the signature of correctness. The McGucken Principle exhibits this property for the canonical commutation relation, the Schrödinger equation, the Born rule, and (as developed across [MG-Foundations, MG-PathInt, MG-Lagrangian]) for substantial portions of quantum-theoretical content. This is the load-bearing structural feature on which the McGucken corpus's derivational power rests.

The Novelty Identified

## Categorical Novelty: Proofs of the Four Irreducibility Propositions

This section provides the proofs of the formal-distinction propositions (source 7.5) using the structural machinery developed in §sec:hamiltonian--§sec:equivalence.

### Irreducibility to single-channel algebraic-symmetry frameworks

**Proposition (Source 7.5, Proposition 1: irreducibility to F_{alg}).** There is no functor Φ: MQF → F_{alg} from the category of McGucken Quantum Formalisms to any single-channel algebraic-symmetry framework F_{alg} (Definition§def:Falg) preserving the structural-overdetermination property.

*Proof.* Suppose for contradiction that such Φ exists. By Definition§def:Falg, the foundational data of F_{alg} are (A1) Hilbert space, (A2) operator algebra, (A3) symmetry-group representation. Propagation-content (Huygens-wavefront, path-space, action principle) is not part of the foundational data of F_{alg}.

The structural-overdetermination property (Lemma§lem:overdet) requires that [, ] = iℏ 1 be reachable from the foundational principle by two routes through disjoint intermediate machinery, with one route using algebraic-symmetry machinery (Stone, Stone--vN) and the other using geometric-propagation machinery (Huygens, path-space, Gaussian short-time).

Under Φ, the geometric-propagation machinery of the Lagrangian route would map to data inside F_{alg}. But F_{alg}'s foundational data include only (A1)--(A3) and lack propagation-content as foundational. Therefore the image of the Lagrangian route under Φ either (i) does not exist (the Lagrangian route is not preserved), or (ii) collapses to algebraic-symmetry data --- in which case the disjointness from the Hamiltonian route is lost, the two routes coincide, and structural overdetermination fails.

In either case, Φ does not preserve the structural-overdetermination property. Contradiction.  ∎

**Corollary (Specific examples, source 7.5).** The McGucken Quantum Formalism is not equivalent to any of: Heisenberg matrix mechanics, Stone--von Neumann, the Wightman framework, the Haag--Kastler framework, the Wigner classification, the Coleman--Mandula theorem, Weinberg reconstruction, Adler trace dynamics --- each of which is in F_{alg} by Definition§def:Falg.

### Irreducibility to single-channel geometric-propagation frameworks

**Proposition (Source 7.5, Proposition 2: irreducibility to F_{prop}).** There is no functor Φ: MQF → F_{prop} to any single-channel geometric-propagation framework (Definition§def:Fprop) preserving the structural-overdetermination property.

*Proof.* Mirror of Proposition§prop:irred-alg. The foundational data of F_{prop} are (P1) configuration manifold, (P2) propagation rule, (P3) action functional. Algebraic-symmetry content is not part of the foundational data. Under Φ, the algebraic-symmetry machinery of the Hamiltonian route (Stone, Stone--vN) maps to data inside F_{prop}, which has no foundational algebraic-symmetry content; the image either fails to exist or collapses to propagation-data. Structural overdetermination fails.  ∎

**Corollary (Specific examples, source 7.5).** The McGucken Quantum Formalism is not equivalent to any of: Feynman path integral as standalone foundation, Bohmian mechanics, Nelson stochastic mechanics, Lindgren--Liukkonen, 't Hooft cellular automaton, Hestenes spacetime algebra, Schuller constructive gravity, standard twistor theory (Penrose 1967, Witten 1978/2003/2004, Berkovits--Witten 2004, Woit 2021) --- each of which is in F_{prop} by Definition§def:Fprop.

### Irreducibility to spectral-triple/categorical-QFT frameworks

**Proposition (Source 7.5, Proposition 3: irreducibility to spectral-triple/categorical-QFT input).** The McGucken Quantum Formalism is not equivalent (in the sense of Propositions§prop:irred-alg, §prop:irred-prop) to Connes's spectral-triple framework or to the Atiyah--Segal--Lurie categorical-QFT framework with their dual-channel content treated as input.

*Proof.* Connes's spectral-triple framework (, ℋ, D) exhibits dual-channel structure with the algebra  encoding algebraic-symmetry content and the Dirac operator D encoding geometric-propagation content (Proposition§prop:connes-input). The categorical-QFT framework's cobordism functor exhibits dual-channel structure with vector-space assignments encoding algebra and cobordism morphisms encoding geometry (Proposition§prop:cat-QFT-input).

In both, the dual-channel content is input data of the framework: (, ℋ, D) is given for Connes, the cobordism functor is given for Atiyah--Segal--Lurie. The dual-channel content is not derived from a single foundational principle; it is a structural feature of the formalism's chosen input data.

In the McGucken Quantum Formalism, the dual-channel content is derived from a single foundational principle (Axiom§ax:flow, the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic) via the disjoint Hamiltonian and Lagrangian routes. This derivation is the structural-overdetermination property (Lemma§lem:overdet).

A functor Φ from MQF to Connes or Atiyah--Segal--Lurie that preserves the dual-channel content would, on the source side, have access to the structural-overdetermination derivation; on the target side, the dual-channel content is input rather than derived, so the derivation has no image. Hence Φ cannot preserve the structural-overdetermination property: the latter is a derivation, the target lacks the derivational structure to realize it.  ∎

### The McGucken Quantum Formalism is a new categorical entity

**Proposition (Source 7.5, Proposition 4: categorical novelty).** The McGucken Quantum Formalism is not equivalent (in the categorical sense of Propositions§prop:irred-alg--§prop:irred-cat) to any quantum-theoretical framework in the comprehensive prior-art survey of §sec:survey-2--§sec:survey-7.

*Proof.* Every framework cataloged in §sec:survey-2--§sec:survey-7 falls into one of three categories:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt,label=()]
2. single-channel algebraic-symmetry F_{alg} (Definition§def:Falg): Heisenberg, Stone--vN, Wightman, Haag--Kastler, Wigner, Coleman--Mandula, Weinberg, Adler;
3. single-channel geometric-propagation F_{prop} (Definition§def:Fprop): Feynman, Dirac, Bohm, Nelson, Lindgren--Liukkonen, 't Hooft, Hestenes, Schuller, Penrose--Witten--Woit twistor;
4. dual-channel-as-input: Connes spectral triple, Atiyah--Segal--Lurie categorical QFT.

By Proposition§prop:irred-alg, MQF is not equivalent to any framework in (1).
By Proposition§prop:irred-prop, MQF is not equivalent to any framework in (2).
By Proposition§prop:irred-cat, MQF is not equivalent to any framework in (3).

Therefore MQF is not equivalent to any framework in the comprehensive prior-art survey. The McGucken Quantum Formalism is a categorically novel quantum-theoretical entity.  ∎

## What Is Novel and What Is Inherited from Prior Art (Source 13--14)

This section audits source 13--14, distinguishing the inherited mathematical apparatus from the structurally novel commitments.

### Mathematical apparatus inherited from prior art

The McGucken Quantum Formalism does not invent new mathematics in the technical sense. It uses well-established mathematical apparatus, all of which is inherited from prior art and given full credit:

- [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=2pt]
- separable complex Hilbert spaces (Hilbert 1900s, Banach 1932, von Neumann 1932 [vonNeumann1932]);
- self-adjoint operators on Hilbert spaces and the spectral theorem (Hilbert, Stone, von Neumann);
- strongly continuous unitary one-parameter groups and Stone's theorem (Stone 1930 [Stone1930]);
- the Stone--von Neumann uniqueness theorem (Stone 1930 [Stone1930], von Neumann 1931 [vonNeumann1931], Mackey 1949 [Mackey1949]);
- Lorentzian four-manifolds, foliations, and time-orientation (Riemann, Levi-Civita, Wheeler);
- the Minkowski metric and its signature (Minkowski 1908);
- the Schrödinger equation (Schrödinger 1926 [Schrodinger1926]);
- the Feynman path integral (Feynman 1948 [Feynman1948]);
- Wiener-type measures on path spaces (Wiener 1923, Kac 1949, Glimm--Jaffe 1987 [GlimmJaffe1987]);
- Gaussian integration and short-time-propagator computations (Feynman 1948 [Feynman1948]);
- Wigner's classification of unitary irreducible Poincaré representations (Wigner 1939 [Wigner1939]);
- the Wightman axioms and the Haag--Kastler axioms for QFT [Wightman1956,GardingWightman1964,StreaterWightman1964,HaagKastler1964,Haag1996];
- the Osterwalder--Schrader Euclidean QFT axiomatization [OsterwalderSchrader1973,OsterwalderSchrader1975];
- Wick rotation as a calculational technique (Wick 1954 [Wick1954]);
- the canonical commutation relations and their representation theory.

This inheritance is to be expected: a foundational principle does not require new mathematics in order to derive established physical results. It requires that established mathematics be re-grounded on a deeper principle. The McGucken Quantum Formalism does precisely this: it derives the established mathematical apparatus of quantum theory from a single foundational principle.

### Structural commitments that are novel

What is novel in the McGucken Quantum Formalism is the structural commitment to dual-channel content from a single foundational principle, with six specific structural commitments:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=4pt,label=(N)]
2. **The McGucken Principle as a physical, geometric flow.** The axiom dx₄/dt = ic is a physical, geometric flow, not a postulate, axiom, or interpretation. The integrated form x₄ = ict is the coordinate consequence of the flow. This re-grounding of the Minkowski formula as flow rather than as algebraic identity is a structural commitment not present in any prior art (Remark§rem:flow-primacy).
3. **Dual-channel content from a single foundational principle.** The McGucken Principle simultaneously specifies algebraic-symmetry content (rate invariance) and geometric-propagation content (spherical symmetry) as parallel sibling consequences of a single foundational principle. No prior framework realizes this structural commitment; this is the categorical novelty established formally in Proposition§prop:cat-novel.
4. **Structural overdetermination as foundational diagnostic.** The canonical commutation relation, the Schrödinger equation, the Born rule, and other quantum-theoretical identities are reached by the McGucken Principle through two disjoint routes. Structural overdetermination is treated as a foundational diagnostic: a correct foundational principle generates fundamental identities through multiple independent chains, not just one (Lemma§lem:overdet, Remark§rem:flow-primacy).
5. **The imaginary unit i as a geometric perpendicularity marker.** The factor i in dx₄/dt = ic marks the perpendicularity of x₄ to the three spatial axes (Remark§rem:i-perp); every appearance of i in quantum theory traces to this geometric origin (Theorems§thm:twelve-i, §thm:meta-i). This is closer to Hestenes's geometric reading of i than to any other prior art, but distinct in its identification of i with a single coordinate axis rather than a four-grade pseudoscalar (Proposition§prop:hestenes-static).
6. **The Wick rotation as a coordinate identification, not a calculational device.** The substitution t → -iτ is the coordinate identification τ = x₄/c, derivable from the integrated McGucken Principle in one step (Theorem§thm:wick-coord). The seventy-year mystery of why the Wick rotation works is dissolved: it is the McGucken Principle written in τ-notation. Every standard application (Schrödinger--diffusion, Euclidean convergence, +iε, factor-of-i unification, reflection positivity, KMS, Gibbons--Hawking, Hawking temperature, Kontsevich--Segal admissibility) is a theorem of dx₄/dt = ic (Theorems§thm:wick-coord--§thm:wick-necessity).
7. **Twistor space CP³ as the geometry of x₄'s expansion.** Penrose's twistor space, the deepest geometric reformulation of relativistic physics in the prior literature, is identified as the geometry of the fourth dimension expanding at the velocity of light (Theorem§thm:CP3-identification). The five Penrose problems and the four open problems Witten flagged across 1978--2004 dissolve under this identification (Propositions§prop:resolve-P1--§prop:chirality-googly). Twistor theory becomes the Lagrangian-channel geometric arena of the McGucken Quantum Formalism.

### In plain language

What is new is not a piece of mathematics. What is new is a structural commitment: that quantum theory descends from a single physical, geometric flow, with both its algebraic-symmetry content and its geometric-propagation content as parallel sibling theorems. Six specific commitments instantiate this overall structure. Each is novel relative to the prior art surveyed in §sec:survey-2--§sec:survey-7; together they constitute the categorical novelty of the McGucken Quantum Formalism (Proposition§prop:cat-novel).

## The Structural Argument for Necessity of the Categorical Novelty (Source 15)

This section audits source 15.

The categorical novelty of §sec:cat-novelty is not optional decoration. It is the load-bearing structural feature on which the McGucken corpus's derivational power rests. The structural argument for this claim:

1. [leftmargin=2em,itemsep=4pt,label=.]
2. **The McGucken corpus's derivational power is empirically substantial.** The McGucken Principle has been shown to generate, as theorems, the Schrödinger equation, the Dirac equation, the canonical commutation relation, the Born rule, the de Broglie relation, the Feynman path integral, the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian formulations as parallel sibling consequences, the Standard Model gauge structure with broken symmetries, the McGucken Lagrangian as unique under fourteen distinct mathematical optimality theorems, the Klein--Erlangen pairing of algebraic-symmetry and geometric-propagation content, the entire suite of standard general-relativistic predictions (Schwarzschild metric, Einstein field equations, Newtonian gravity), the second law of thermodynamics, the entropy increase in cosmology, the arrow of time, and the structure of the Wick rotation [MG-Foundations, MG-PathInt, MG-SM, MG-Lagrangian, MG-LagrangianOpt, MG-KleinianConstructor, MG-Wick, MG-Entropy, MG-Eleven, MG-GR, MG-GR, MG-Newton, MG-Twistor, MG-WittenTwistor].
3. **The derivational power requires both channels.** The Hamiltonian-route results (canonical commutation, Stone--vN uniqueness, operator-algebraic structure) require the algebraic-symmetry channel (McGucken Channel A). The Lagrangian-route results (Feynman path integral, action principle, Huygens' principle, geometric-propagation content) require the geometric-propagation channel (McGucken Channel B). The structural-overdetermination results (canonical commutation reached by both routes, equivalence of formulations as a structural theorem) require both channels jointly. No single-channel framework can reproduce the full derivational power.
4. **The derivational power requires the channels to descend from a single principle.** If algebraic and propagation channels were independent inputs (as in Connes's spectral triple or Atiyah--Segal--Lurie categorical QFT), the structural-overdetermination property would be lost: the canonical commutation would be reached by one chain, the path integral by another, but the chains would not be derivations from a common foundational principle. The structural diagnostic that identifies dx₄/dt = ic as the correct foundation would not function. Counterfactual modifications of the principle (Lemma§lem:overdet) would not propagate consistently to both channels. The derivational power's robustness under principle-modification (sensitivity-of-the-correct-principle test) requires the principle to be the single source of both channels.
5. **Conclusion of the structural argument.** The categorical novelty of the McGucken Quantum Formalism (a single physical principle generating dual-channel content with structural-overdetermination) is the structural feature on which the corpus's derivational power rests. It is not optional decoration. Removing it would collapse the framework into one of the surveyed categories (1)--(3) of Proposition§prop:cat-novel, with corresponding loss of derivational reach. The categorical novelty is load-bearing.

**Remark (The counterfactual evaporation test).** A standard test of whether a structural feature is load-bearing is the counterfactual evaporation test: would the framework's empirical content survive removal of the feature? For the McGucken Quantum Formalism's dual-channel structural commitment, the answer is no: removing the dual-channel commitment removes structural overdetermination (no two-route derivation of the canonical commutator); removing structural overdetermination removes the foundational diagnostic that selects dx₄/dt = ic over alternative principles; removing the foundational diagnostic leaves a framework that is empirically arbitrary among many possible foundations. The dual-channel commitment is therefore load-bearing in the strictest sense.

### In plain language (verbatim from source 14)

> Could the work of the McGucken corpus be done in any prior single-channel framework? Could the Hamiltonian--Lagrangian dual derivation, the seven-duality structure, the structural-overdetermination of the canonical commutation relation be reproduced in Heisenberg--Stone--von Neumann, Feynman path integral, Bohmian mechanics, Connes spectral triple, or Atiyah--Segal categorical QFT? The answer is no. Each of these is single-channel or treats the dual-channel content as input rather than as derived. The McGucken Quantum Formalism's derivational power requires the dual-channel content as a structural commitment. The categorical novelty is not optional decoration; it is the structural foundation of the framework.

## Abstract

We herein present a novel quantum-theoretical framework in the form of the McGucken Quantum Formalism (MQF). This paper conducts a comprehensive survey of the prior art in quantum theory and related fields and formally demonstrates that the McGucken Quantum Formalism has never before been realized throughout the mathematical and physical literature of quantum mechanics, quantum field theory, the Standard Model apparatus, and the major foundational programs. The McGucken Quantum Formalism is the quantum-theoretical structure underlying the Physical McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic, which states that the fourth dimension of spacetime is itself an active geometric process expanding spherically from every spacetime event at the velocity of light. The framework’s defining structural feature is its dual-channel content: the geometric statement dx₄/dt = ic simultaneously specifies an algebraic-symmetry channel (the invariance of x₄’s advance under time translation, space translation, rotation, and Lorentz boost) and a geometric-propagation channel (the spherical symmetry of x₄’s expansion as Huygens-wavefront propagation from every spacetime event). The two channels generate the Hamiltonian operator formulation and the Lagrangian path-integral formulation of quantum mechanics as independent theorems through disjoint intermediate structures — the structural overdetermination of the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ first established in [MG-Foundations, 2026].

The companion paper [MG-Geometry, 2026] establishes that the McGucken Geometry is a categorically novel geometric category not equivalent to any framework of dynamical geometry mainstream physics has accommodated from 1915 to 2026. The present paper extends that result to the quantum-theoretical sector. The comprehensive survey covers the major frameworks for foundational quantum theory: the Stone-von Neumann theorem and the Heisenberg/Schrödinger uniqueness apparatus [1, 2]; Feynman’s 1948 path integral [3]; Dirac’s 1933 transformation theory [4]; Wightman’s axiomatic QFT [5, 6]; Haag-Kastler algebraic QFT [7]; Osterwalder-Schrader Euclidean QFT [8]; Connes’s noncommutative geometry and the spectral-triple formulation of the Standard Model [9, 10]; geometric quantization (Kostant 1970, Souriau 1970, Woodhouse) [11, 12, 13]; Hestenes’s spacetime algebra and geometric calculus [14, 15]; Adler’s trace dynamics [16]; Bohmian mechanics [17, 18]; Nelson’s stochastic mechanics [19, 20]; Lindgren-Liukkonen stochastic optimal control [21]; ‘t Hooft’s cellular automaton interpretation [22]; Witten’s twistor string [23]; Schuller’s constructive gravity [24]; Woit’s Euclidean twistor unification [25]; Wigner’s 1939 classification of Poincaré representations [26]; categorical and topological QFT (Atiyah-Segal, Lurie, Baez-Dolan) [27, 28, 29]; Yang-Mills gauge theory and the principal-bundle apparatus [30]; renormalization-group methods (Wilson, Polchinski) [31, 32]; Coleman-Mandula and Weinberg-reconstruction theorems [33, 34]; and the categorical and Erlangen-program foundations of physics [35]. Across this comprehensive survey, the central question is examined: in any of these prior frameworks, is there a quantum-theoretical structure realizing the dual-channel commitment of the McGucken Principle — algebraic-symmetry content and geometric-propagation content as parallel sibling consequences of a single foundational geometric postulate? The answer, established by direct examination of each framework in turn, is no. Each of the surveyed frameworks realizes one channel or the other; none realizes both as parallel sibling consequences of a single principle.

The paper is organized in four parts following the architecture of the companion McGucken Geometry paper [MG-Geometry]. Part I (Comprehensive Survey: §§1-7) catalogs the prior frameworks of quantum theory across operator-algebraic, path-integral, axiomatic-QFT, geometric-quantization, spacetime-algebraic, stochastic, and categorical-QFT branches. Part II (Formal Distinction: §7.4) establishes through formal definitions, propositions, and proofs that the McGucken Quantum Formalism is not reducible to any single-channel framework, and that the dual-channel category is genuinely categorically novel. Part III (Formal Definition: §§8-12) presents the McGucken Quantum Formalism in three equivalent presentations: the dual-channel triple (M, F, V; H, A, ψ) of §9; the operator-algebraic formalization of §10; the path-integral formalization of §11; and the equivalence theorem of §12 establishing the three formulations as mathematically equivalent through the structural-overdetermination route. Part IV (The Novelty Identified: §§13-17) identifies what is mathematically novel and what is taken from prior art, articulates the structural inference that the framework supplies a new quantum-theoretical category — dual-channel quantum theory, the quantum theory of frameworks with simultaneous algebraic-symmetry and geometric-propagation content — and develops the structural argument for why this novelty is not optional decoration but the load-bearing content from which the McGucken corpus’s derivational power descends.

The paper’s thesis can be stated in three sentences. First: the mathematical apparatus needed to formalize dual-channel quantum theory has been available across the operator-algebraic, path-integral, geometric-quantization, and noncommutative-geometric literatures since the foundational decades of quantum theory; the comprehensive survey gives full credit to von Neumann (1932), Stone (1930), Wigner (1939), Feynman (1948), Dirac (1933), Wightman (1956), Haag-Kastler (1964), Connes (1985), and the entire subsequent literature. Second: the dual-channel content of the McGucken Principle has been mathematically inescapable since Minkowski’s 1908 formula x₄ = ict; the formula contains both the invariance content (the constancy of the rate ic) and the spherical-symmetric propagation content (the geometric structure of x₄’s expansion) as parallel sibling features. Third: the willingness to commit to the dual-channel content as a structural commitment of quantum theory — rather than dismissing it as a coincidence of mathematical formulation — is what was missing, and what the McGucken Quantum Formalism supplies. The mathematical apparatus has been there. The dual-channel quantum-theoretical category has not been articulated in any prior framework, as the comprehensive survey of §§2-7 establishes by direct examination.

**Keywords:** McGucken Quantum Formalism; MQF; dual-channel quantum theory; McGucken Principle; dx₄/dt = ic; Stone-von Neumann theorem; Heisenberg picture; Schrödinger picture; Feynman path integral; canonical commutation relation; structural overdetermination; Wightman axioms; Haag-Kastler algebraic QFT; noncommutative geometry; spectral triple; geometric quantization; Hestenes spacetime algebra; Wigner classification; Coleman-Mandula; Weinberg reconstruction; categorical QFT; Atiyah-Segal-Lurie; algebraic-symmetry channel; geometric-propagation channel; comprehensive prior-art survey; companion to McGucken Geometry.

## 1. Introduction: The Methodology and the Question

The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic asserts that the fourth dimension of spacetime is itself an active geometric process expanding spherically from every spacetime event at the velocity of light. The companion paper [MG-Geometry, 2026] establishes that this assertion defines a categorically novel geometric category, moving-dimension geometry, not equivalent to any framework of dynamical geometry mainstream physics has accommodated from 1915 to 2026. The companion paper’s analysis is centered on differential geometry and on the major frameworks of dynamical geometry: general relativity, inflationary cosmology, gravitational-wave physics, FLRW cosmology, Einstein-aether theory, Hořava-Lifshitz gravity, Causal Dynamical Triangulations, Shape Dynamics.

The present paper extends the analysis to the quantum-theoretical sector. The Physical McGucken Principle informs and is in turn formalized by quantum mechanics, quantum electrodynamics, quantum field theory, the Standard Model apparatus, and the major foundational programs — each with its own developed mathematical structure, its own formal apparatus, and its own categorical commitments. The substantial McGucken-corpus literature [MG-Foundations, MG-PathIntegral, MG-StandardModel, MG-Lagrangian, MG-LagrangianOpt, MG-KleinianConstructor] has demonstrated that the McGucken Principle generates substantial portions of quantum-theoretical content as theorems: the Schrödinger equation, the Dirac equation, the canonical commutation relation, the Born rule, the de Broglie relation, the Feynman path integral, the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian formulations as parallel sibling consequences, the Standard Model gauge structure with broken symmetries, the McGucken Lagrangian as unique under fourteen distinct mathematical optimality theorems, and the Klein-Erlangen pairing of algebraic-symmetry and geometric-propagation content.

The question addressed by the present paper is not whether dx₄/dt = ic generates these results — the McGucken corpus has established that it does — but whether the resulting quantum-theoretical framework, the McGucken Quantum Formalism, is categorically novel relative to the prior art of quantum theory. The methodology mirrors that of the companion paper: comprehensive prior-art survey followed by formal-distinction propositions. Each prior framework is examined directly; its central content is articulated; the structural difference from the McGucken Quantum Formalism is identified. The conclusion of the survey is then established by formal proposition with proof: the McGucken Quantum Formalism is not equivalent to any prior quantum-theoretical framework in the comprehensive survey, in the formal sense that there is no functor preserving the structural commitments of dual-channel content.

**The dual-channel structural commitment.** The defining feature of the McGucken Quantum Formalism, identified in [MG-Foundations] and developed formally throughout the present paper, is its dual-channel content. The geometric statement dx₄/dt = ic simultaneously specifies two logically distinct pieces of information: an algebraic-symmetry content (the invariance of x₄’s advance at rate ic under time translation, space translation, rotation, and Lorentz boost) and a geometric-propagation content (the spherical symmetry of x₄’s expansion as Huygens-wavefront propagation from every spacetime event). The two contents are the two faces of one Kleinian object; the McGucken-Erlangen pairing of [MG-KleinianConstructor] establishes their formal correspondence. The dual-channel content is the structural feature that makes the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian formulations of quantum mechanics two parallel theorems of the single principle dx₄/dt = ic, with the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ derived through two disjoint intermediate routes — the structural-overdetermination property of [MG-Foundations].

**In plain language.** The paper’s methodology is exactly the methodology of the companion McGucken Geometry paper, applied to the quantum side. We survey the prior art of quantum theory deeply and give every prior framework its full credit. We then ask: does any of them realize the dual-channel content of the McGucken Principle — algebraic-symmetry content and geometric-propagation content as parallel sibling consequences of a single foundational principle? The answer, established by direct examination, is no. The mathematical apparatuses of operator-algebraic QM, path-integral QM, axiomatic QFT, noncommutative geometry, geometric quantization, and the rest are all in the prior art, given full credit. The dual-channel quantum-theoretical category they would together populate has not been articulated.

## 2. The Operator-Algebraic Foundation: Stone-von Neumann and Heisenberg-Schrödinger Equivalence

### **2.1 Heisenberg 1925 and Matrix Mechanics**

Werner Heisenberg’s 1925 paper [36] introduced matrix mechanics as the first complete formulation of quantum mechanics. The fundamental structures are the matrix-valued operators q̂ and p̂ satisfying the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ, with the time evolution governed by the Heisenberg equation of motion d̃A/dt = (i/ℏ)[Ĥ, Â]. The Heisenberg picture treats operators as time-dependent and states as time-independent. Born and Jordan’s 1925 paper [37] supplied the matrix-mechanical formulation; Born, Heisenberg, and Jordan’s 1926 ‘Drei-Männer-Arbeit’ [38] extended the formalism to multiple degrees of freedom.

The Heisenberg apparatus is operator-algebraic: its content is the algebra of operators on a Hilbert space, with the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ as the foundational algebraic identity. This is the algebraic-symmetry content of quantum theory in its pure form: invariance under time translation generates the Hamiltonian; the Hamiltonian generates the Heisenberg equation; the canonical commutation relation closes the operator algebra. The Heisenberg picture has a single channel: algebraic-symmetry content with no geometric-propagation content.

### **2.2 Schrödinger 1926 and Wave Mechanics**

Erwin Schrödinger’s 1926 papers [39] introduced wave mechanics as an alternative formulation of quantum mechanics. The fundamental structure is the wave function ψ(x, t) on configuration space, satisfying the Schrödinger equation iℏ∂ψ/∂t = Ĥψ. The Schrödinger picture treats states as time-dependent and operators (excluding the Hamiltonian) as time-independent. Schrödinger’s wave mechanics arose from a different mathematical setting (configuration-space wave functions, partial differential equations) than Heisenberg’s matrix mechanics.

The Schrödinger picture, in its 1926 formulation, is closer to the geometric-propagation channel than the Heisenberg picture: the wave function ψ(x, t) propagates through configuration space; the Schrödinger equation is a wave equation in the configuration-space sense. However, the Schrödinger picture does not, by itself, supply the geometric mechanism for the propagation: the Hamiltonian is given as input, the wave function is given as initial data, and the propagation is what the equation calculates. The Schrödinger picture is single-channel in that it has the propagation content but lacks an algebraic-symmetry derivation of the Hamiltonian; the Hamiltonian is a postulate, not a theorem.

### **2.3 The Stone-von Neumann Theorem (1930-1932)**

The equivalence of Heisenberg matrix mechanics and Schrödinger wave mechanics was established by the Stone-von Neumann theorem of 1930-1932 [1, 2]. Marshall Stone’s 1930 paper [40] and John von Neumann’s 1931 paper [1] together prove that the irreducible unitary representations of the Heisenberg group on n generators are unique up to unitary equivalence: every two irreducible representations of the canonical commutation relations are unitarily equivalent. As a consequence, the matrix-mechanical and wave-mechanical formulations of quantum mechanics are unitarily equivalent — they are two presentations of the same underlying mathematical structure.

The Stone-von Neumann theorem [1, 2, 41] is the deepest structural result on the operator-algebraic foundation of quantum mechanics. It establishes the categorical equivalence of the Heisenberg and Schrödinger pictures and supplies the mathematical apparatus for Mackey’s 1949 generalization [42] to representations of Heisenberg groups associated to locally compact abelian groups, Rieffel’s 1972 work on the uniqueness of the Heisenberg commutation relations [43], and the entire subsequent operator-algebraic literature on quantization.

The Stone-von Neumann framework, however, does not address the question of why [q̂, p̂] = iℏ. The canonical commutation relation is an input to the Stone-von Neumann theorem, not an output. The theorem asserts that, given the relation, its irreducible unitary representations are unique; it does not derive the relation from a deeper structural commitment. The Heisenberg-Schrödinger equivalence is therefore a structural fact about a postulated algebraic identity, not a derivation of the algebraic identity from a foundational principle. The McGucken Quantum Formalism, in [MG-Foundations, Propositions H.1-H.5], derives the canonical commutation relation from dx₄/dt = ic through the Hamiltonian route; the Stone-von Neumann uniqueness theorem then enters as Proposition H.5 to close the representation.

### **2.4 von Neumann’s 1932 Mathematische Grundlagen**

John von Neumann’s 1932 monograph “Mathematische Grundlagen der Quantenmechanik” [44] supplies the standard reference for the operator-algebraic foundations of quantum mechanics. The framework comprises: a separable complex Hilbert space H of state vectors; self-adjoint operators on H representing observables; unitary evolution generated by the Hamiltonian; the Born rule for measurement probabilities; the projection postulate for measurement updates; and the spectral theorem decomposing self-adjoint operators into spectral measures.

The von Neumann framework is the canonical operator-algebraic apparatus of quantum mechanics. It is purely algebraic-symmetry in character: the Hilbert space is given, the operators act, the symmetries are encoded in the operator algebra, and the propagation is a derived consequence of the Hamiltonian’s spectral structure. The framework does not ground its structural commitments in any geometric content; the Hilbert space is supplied as a mathematical setting, the operators are postulated, and the geometric content of x₄ — the privileged temporal axis — is absent from the framework as anything but a coordinate label on the wave function.

**In plain language.** The Heisenberg-Schrödinger-Stone-von Neumann apparatus of operator-algebraic quantum mechanics has been the standard mathematical foundation of quantum theory since the 1930s. It is given full credit.
