With dx₄/dt = ic and using the relation between x₄ and proper time τ (with x₄ = icτ for proper time), the accumulated phase is exp(-mc²Δτ/ℏ) for a path of proper-time interval Δτ. In the non-relativistic limit, this becomes exp(iS[x(t)]/ℏ) where S[x(t)] is the classical action along the path.

**Proposition L.4 (Feynman path integral).** The sum over all paths in the path space, weighted by the x₄-phase exp(iS[x(t)]/ℏ), reproduces the Feynman path integral K(x_B, t_B; x_A, t_A) = ∫ 𝒟[x(t)] exp(iS[x(t)]/ℏ).

**Proposition L.5 (Schrödinger equation).** Gaussian integration of the short-time propagator in the Feynman path integral, in the limit Δt → 0, yields the Schrödinger equation iℏ∂ψ/∂t = Ĥψ. The derivation is Feynman’s standard 1948 derivation, adapted to the McGucken-geometric setting.

**Proposition L.6 (Canonical commutation relation by direct computation).** From the Schrödinger equation derived in Proposition L.5, direct computation of [q̂, p̂] in the configuration-space representation reproduces [q̂, p̂] = iℏ through the same calculation as Proposition H.4.

The Lagrangian route therefore proceeds: (L.1) dx₄/dt = ic forces Huygens’ Principle. (L.2) Iterated Huygens expansions generate path space. (L.3) x₄-phase along a path equals the classical action. (L.4) Sum over paths weighted by phase gives the Feynman path integral. (L.5) Gaussian-integration short-time limit gives the Schrödinger equation. (L.6) Direct computation gives [q̂, p̂] = iℏ. The route uses standard prior art — Huygens (1678), Feynman (1948), and direct computation — combined with the McGucken Principle’s identification of x₄-expansion with Huygens propagation.

**Disjoint intermediate machinery.** The crucial structural feature, established in [MG-Foundations, Theorem VII.1] and central to the dual-channel content of the McGucken Quantum Formalism, is that the Hamiltonian route (Propositions H.1-H.5) and the Lagrangian route (Propositions L.1-L.6) share no intermediate machinery except the starting principle dx₄/dt = ic and the final algebraic identity [q̂, p̂] = iℏ. The Hamiltonian route uses Stone’s theorem on translation-invariance, the configuration representation, and the Stone-von Neumann uniqueness theorem; the Lagrangian route uses Huygens-wavefront propagation, path-space generation, and Gaussian short-time integration. The two sets of intermediate steps are disjoint. The same canonical commutation relation is reached by two independent chains. This is the structural overdetermination of the dual-channel content.

## 12. The Equivalence Theorem

***Theorem 12.1 (MQF Equivalence Theorem).*** *Let (M, F, V) be a moving-dimension manifold satisfying [MG-Geometry, Definition 9.3]. The following three structures on (M, F, V) are mathematically equivalent presentations of the McGucken Quantum Formalism:*

**(i)** A dual-channel sextuple (M, F, V; H, A, ψ) of Definition 9.2, with quantum layer (H, A, ψ) satisfying (Q1)-(Q4).

**(ii)** An operator-algebraic presentation: a separable Hilbert space H carrying an irreducible unitary representation of the Heisenberg group (associated to the spatial axes of (M, F, V) and a chosen non-zero real parameter ℏ), with the time evolution generated by a Hamiltonian Ĥ producing the Schrödinger equation iℏ∂ψ/∂t = Ĥψ.

**(iii)** A path-integral presentation: the Feynman propagator K(x_B, t_B; x_A, t_A) = ∫ 𝒟[x(t)] exp(iS[x(t)]/ℏ) over all continuous paths in (M, F, V) from (x_A, t_A) to (x_B, t_B), with the action S[x(t)] given by the McGucken Lagrangian ℒ_McG.

The three presentations of the McGucken Quantum Formalism — the dual-channel sextuple, the operator-algebraic Heisenberg-group representation, and the path-integral Feynman propagator — are equivalent in the sense that each can be derived from each of the others through standard quantum-theoretical machinery, and all three descend from the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic through the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian routes of §§10-11.

**Proof sketch.** The equivalences follow from the structural-overdetermination property [MG-Foundations, Theorem VII.1]. (i) ⇒ (ii): The dual-channel sextuple specifies (H, A, ψ) directly; the operator-algebraic structure (Q2) is the Heisenberg-group representation. (i) ⇒ (iii): The dual-channel sextuple’s Lagrangian-channel content (Q3) is the wave-function evolution; the path-integral presentation is the Feynman-Lagrangian formulation of this evolution via Propositions L.1-L.5. (ii) ⇔ (iii): The Stone-von Neumann theorem and Feynman’s 1948 path-integral derivation of the Schrödinger equation establish the equivalence of the two standard quantum-mechanical formulations. The McGucken Principle’s structural overdetermination supplies the deeper reason for this equivalence: both presentations descend from dx₄/dt = ic through disjoint routes. ■

## 13. What Is Novel and What Is Not

### **13.1 Mathematical Apparatus Taken From Prior Art**

The mathematical apparatus used throughout the formal definitions of §§9-12 is taken directly from the prior literature surveyed in §§2-7. Each is given full credit.

**Hilbert spaces and self-adjoint operators.** Standard reference: von Neumann 1932 [44]. The Hilbert-space apparatus underlying the operator-algebraic content of MQF is the standard apparatus of quantum mechanics.

**Stone’s theorem on one-parameter unitary groups.** Stone 1930 [40]. The momentum-generator derivation of Proposition H.2 uses Stone’s theorem directly.

**The Stone-von Neumann uniqueness theorem.** Stone 1930 [40], von Neumann 1931 [1], 1932 [2]. The closure of the Hamiltonian-route representation of Proposition H.5 uses the Stone-von Neumann uniqueness theorem directly.

**The canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ.** Heisenberg, Born, Jordan 1925 [36, 37, 38]. The relation itself is standard quantum mechanics; the McGucken contribution is its dual-route derivation, not the relation.

**The Schrödinger equation.** Schrödinger 1926 [39]. The equation is standard quantum mechanics; the McGucken contribution is its derivation from dx₄/dt = ic via both routes.

**The Feynman path integral.** Feynman 1948 [3], with Dirac 1933 [4] antecedent. The path-integral formalism is Feynman’s; the McGucken contribution is its derivation from x₄’s spherical-symmetric expansion via Huygens-wavefront propagation.

**Huygens’ Principle.** Huygens 1678. The principle is classical wave optics; the McGucken contribution is its identification with x₄’s spherical-symmetric expansion (Proposition L.1).

**Wightman, Haag-Kastler, Osterwalder-Schrader axiomatic QFT.** Wightman 1956 [5, 6], Haag-Kastler 1964 [7], Osterwalder-Schrader 1973 [8]. These frameworks are inherited as the algebraic-symmetry-channel apparatus of MQF at the QFT level.

**Wigner classification, Coleman-Mandula, Weinberg reconstruction.** Wigner 1939 [26], Coleman-Mandula 1967 [33], Weinberg 1995 [61]. These results are inherited as the representation-theoretic apparatus of MQF’s algebraic-symmetry channel.

**Yang-Mills gauge theory.** Yang-Mills 1954 [62]. The gauge-theoretic apparatus is inherited; the McGucken contribution is the McGucken-Lagrangian derivation [MG-Lagrangian, Proposition VI.2] that forces the Yang-Mills form from local x₄-phase invariance.

**Connes’s spectral triples and noncommutative geometry.** Connes 1985 [9], Connes-Marcolli 2008 [10]. Synthesis with the spectral-triple framework is structurally compatible (cf. §5.3); the framework is inherited as a candidate apparatus for the algebraic-side categorical formulation.

**Categorical QFT and cobordism categories.** Atiyah 1988 [27], Segal 1988 [28], Lurie 2008 [29]. The categorical apparatus is inherited as a setting for dual-channel content; cf. §7.1.

**Wilsonian renormalization group.** Wilson 1971 [31], Polchinski 1984 [32]. The RG apparatus is inherited as the methodological tool for the dimensional-completeness analysis of [MG-Lagrangian, Theorem 4.1].

In summary, every individual piece of mathematical apparatus used in the formalization of MQF exists in prior art and is given full credit above. The mathematical machinery is not new. The novelty resides entirely in the structural commitments combining the machinery into a dual-channel framework with structural overdetermination.

### **13.2 Structural Commitments That Constitute the Novelty**

**Novelty 1: Dual-channel derivation from a single principle.** The McGucken Quantum Formalism derives both the algebraic-symmetry channel and the geometric-propagation channel from a single foundational principle dx₄/dt = ic. The Hamiltonian route (§10) and the Lagrangian route (§11) are parallel sibling derivations from the same principle. No prior framework realizes this dual-channel derivation.

**Novelty 2: Structural overdetermination of the canonical commutation relation.** The canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ is derived through two routes via disjoint intermediate machinery, with the same i and the same ℏ reached by both chains. No prior framework derives a fundamental quantum-theoretical identity through two disjoint routes from a foundational principle.

**Novelty 3: Geometric grounding of the imaginary unit i.** The imaginary unit i appearing in the canonical commutation relation, the Schrödinger equation, the Feynman path integral, and quantum theory generally is grounded in the imaginary character of x₄ in the Minkowski formula x₄ = ict. The factor i is not an external mathematical convenience but a geometric structural feature of the McGucken Principle. Hestenes’s spacetime-algebra framework [14, 15] supplies an alternative geometric grounding of i (as the spacetime pseudoscalar); the McGucken grounding is structurally distinct (i as the imaginary character of x₄).

**Novelty 4: Geometric grounding of ℏ.** The action quantum ℏ appearing in quantum theory is derived from c plus the McGucken-self-consistency principle [MG-Constants], not taken as an external empirical input. The McGucken Lagrangian uniqueness analysis [MG-Lagrangian, Proposition III.3] establishes ℏ as derived from the McGucken-self-consistency requirement.

**Novelty 5: The seven-duality property.** The seven McGucken Dualities of Physics [MG-Dual] — Hamiltonian/Lagrangian, Noether/Second-Law, Heisenberg/Schrödinger, wave/particle, locality/nonlocality, rest mass / spatial motion, time/space — arise as parallel sibling consequences of the dual-channel content of dx₄/dt = ic. No prior Lagrangian framework in the 282-year tradition (Newton 1788 through string theory 1968-present) generates more than two of the seven dualities; the McGucken framework generates all seven [MG-LagrangianOpt, §6.7].

**Novelty 6: Categorical novelty of dual-channel quantum theory.** The category of dual-channel quantum-theoretical frameworks (Definition 7.5.3) is not equivalent to any category of single-channel framework, nor to the standard formulations of spectral-triple or categorical-QFT frameworks (Proposition 7.5.4). The dual-channel quantum-theoretical category is a genuinely new categorical structure relative to the prior art.

## 14. The Structural Argument for the Necessity of the Categorical Novelty

Why is the categorical novelty of dual-channel quantum theory necessary — that is, why does the McGucken corpus require a genuinely new quantum-theoretical category rather than being expressible within an existing one? The structural argument mirrors that of [MG-Geometry, §14] for the geometric side.

### **14.1 The Empirical Content of the McGucken Quantum Corpus**

The McGucken corpus [MG-Proof, MG-Master, MG-PathIntegral, MG-StandardModel, MG-Lagrangian, MG-LagrangianOpt, MG-Foundations, MG-KleinianConstructor, MG-Constants, MG-deBroglie, MG-Uncertainty, MG-Born, MG-Dirac] establishes that the following empirical content of physics descends as theorems from dx₄/dt = ic via dual-channel content: the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ through both routes; the Schrödinger equation through both routes; the Heisenberg and Schrödinger pictures as parallel sibling formulations; the wave-particle duality as the dual-channel reading of x₄-advance at the ontological level; the Feynman path integral as the Lagrangian-channel content; the Born rule from the McGucken Sphere; the de Broglie relation p = h/λ; the Dirac equation; the Standard Model gauge structure; the McGucken Lagrangian as unique under fourteen mathematical optimality theorems; and the seven-duality structure of physics.

### **14.2 The Counterfactual Evaporation Test**

Strip the dual-channel content from the McGucken Quantum Formalism — treat MQF as a single-channel framework with one channel as input and the other as derived — and ask what remains of the McGucken corpus’s derivations.

The answer is that the structural-overdetermination property evaporates. Without the dual-channel content, the canonical commutation relation cannot be derived through two disjoint routes; one of the two routes becomes internal to the framework, and the other route disappears. Without the structural-overdetermination property, the Heisenberg-Schrödinger equivalence is the standard mathematical-equivalence fact rather than a structural-overdetermination signature. Without the seven-duality structure being parallel sibling consequences of one principle, the seven dualities revert to the standard view of physics in which they are independent features that happen to coexist. The counterfactual evaporation establishes that the dual-channel content is the load-bearing structural feature of MQF: it is what gives the framework its derivational power and its categorical novelty.

**In plain language.** Could the work of the McGucken corpus be done in any prior single-channel framework? Could the Hamiltonian-Lagrangian dual derivation, the seven-duality structure, the structural-overdetermination of the canonical commutation relation be reproduced in Heisenberg-Stone-von Neumann, Feynman path integral, Bohmian mechanics, Connes spectral triple, or Atiyah-Segal categorical QFT? The answer is no. Each of these is single-channel or treats the dual-channel content as input rather than as derived. The McGucken Quantum Formalism’s derivational power requires the dual-channel content as a structural commitment. The categorical novelty is not optional decoration; it is the structural foundation of the framework.

## 15. The Structural Overdetermination Lemma

***Lemma 15.1 (Structural Overdetermination of [q̂, p̂] = iℏ).*** *In the McGucken Quantum Formalism, the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ is derivable from the foundational principle dx₄/dt = ic through two independent routes via disjoint intermediate machinery: the Hamiltonian route of Propositions H.1-H.5, and the Lagrangian route of Propositions L.1-L.6. The two routes share no intermediate structure except the starting principle and the final algebraic identity.*

**Proof.** Direct examination of the two routes establishes the disjointness of intermediate machinery. The Hamiltonian route uses: (a) Stone’s theorem on strongly continuous one-parameter unitary groups; (b) the configuration-space representation of the wave function; (c) the Stone-von Neumann uniqueness theorem. The Lagrangian route uses: (a) iterated Huygens-wavefront expansions; (b) path-space generation via discretization in time; (c) Gaussian short-time integration in the Feynman propagator. The two sets of machinery are disjoint: Stone’s theorem does not appear in the Lagrangian route; Huygens-wavefront propagation does not appear in the Hamiltonian route; configuration representation and Gaussian path-integration are different operations. The two routes share only the starting principle dx₄/dt = ic and the final algebraic identity [q̂, p̂] = iℏ. The structural overdetermination is established. ■

The Structural Overdetermination Lemma is the central technical content of the McGucken Quantum Formalism. It establishes the dual-channel commitment of MQF as a mathematical fact about the framework, not merely as a philosophical interpretation. The fact that the canonical commutation relation is reachable by two disjoint routes from the same starting principle is the formal-mathematical content of the dual-channel structural commitment, and it is the property that distinguishes MQF from every prior single-channel framework.

## 16. Source-Paper Provenance: The McGucken Quantum Corpus

The formalization developed in §§9-15 rests on a substantial corpus of prior McGucken-corpus papers, each contributing specific derivational content. The provenance is given here for completeness.

**The foundational derivation.** The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic itself was developed across approximately four decades of work, beginning with the author’s undergraduate work with John Archibald Wheeler at Princeton (1988-1993), continuing through the Foundational Questions Institute (FQXi) papers (2008-2013), the 2016 monograph, and the active derivation program at elliotmcguckenphysics.com (2025-2026). The companion paper [MG-Geometry, 2026] supplies the full derivation chronology.

**The Hamiltonian-Lagrangian dual derivation.** The structural-overdetermination derivation of the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ through both routes is established in [MG-Foundations, 2026]: “The Deeper Foundations of Quantum Mechanics: How the McGucken Principle Uniquely Generates the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian Formulations of Quantum Mechanics” (elliotmcguckenphysics.com, April 23, 2026). The five-proposition Hamiltonian route (H.1-H.5) and the six-proposition Lagrangian route (L.1-L.6) are developed in §§II-III of that paper, with the structural-overdetermination property established as Theorem VII.1.

**The path-integral derivation.** The derivation of Feynman’s path integral from x₄’s spherical-symmetric expansion is established in [MG-PathIntegral, 2026]: “A Derivation of Feynman’s Path Integral from the McGucken Principle” (elliotmcguckenphysics.com, April 15, 2026). The Huygens-wavefront propagation derivation of Proposition L.1 is from this paper.

**The Standard Model derivation.** The derivation of the Standard Model’s broken symmetries (parity, charge-conjugation, CP, T violation), arrows of time, electroweak symmetry breaking, chiral symmetry breaking, baryogenesis, and the strong CP problem from dx₄/dt = ic is established in [MG-StandardModel, 2026]: “How the McGucken Principle Accounts for the Standard Model’s Broken Symmetries, Time’s Arrows and Asymmetries” (elliotmcguckenphysics.com, April 13, 2026).

**The Lagrangian uniqueness theorems.** The four sector-uniqueness theorems for the McGucken Lagrangian ℒ_McG = ℒ_kin + ℒ_Dirac + ℒ_YM + ℒ_EH are established in [MG-Lagrangian, 2026]: “The Unique McGucken Lagrangian: All Four Sectors Forced by the McGucken Principle” (elliotmcguckenphysics.com, April 23, 2026). The triple-optimality (uniqueness, simplicity, completeness) under fourteen mathematical theorems is established in [MG-LagrangianOpt, 2026]: “The McGucken Lagrangian as Unique, Simplest, and Most Complete” (elliotmcguckenphysics.com, April 25, 2026).

**The categorical formalization.** The Klein-Erlangen adjoint-pair formalization, the constructor-theoretic foundation, and the seven-duality 2-categorical specialization diagram are established in [MG-KleinianConstructor, 2026]: “The McGucken-Kleinian Programme as the Geometric Foundation of Constructor Theory: A Categorical Formalization” (elliotmcguckenphysics.com, April 25, 2026).

**The companion geometric paper.** The geometric-side categorical-novelty result — that moving-dimension geometry is a categorically novel geometric category not equivalent to metric dynamics or scale-factor dynamics — is established in [MG-Geometry, 2026]: “McGucken Geometry: The Novel Mathematical Structure of Moving-Dimension Geometry” (the present paper’s companion). The methodology of comprehensive prior-art survey followed by formal-distinction propositions is developed there and applied to the QM/QFT side here.

## 17. Conclusion: A Compliment to Quantum Theory’s Prior Art

The thesis of this paper is that the McGucken Quantum Formalism is the categorically novel quantum-theoretical structure underlying the Physical McGucken Principle of a Fourth Expanding Dimension dx₄/dt = ic. The structure articulates a mathematical commitment — the dual-channel content of foundational quantum theory, with both algebraic-symmetry channel and geometric-propagation channel as parallel sibling consequences of a single foundational principle — that has not been articulated in any prior quantum-theoretical framework, as established by direct examination across §§2-7 and by formal proof in §7.5.

The thesis is, in a sense, the deepest possible compliment to the prior art of quantum theory. The mathematical apparatus of operator-algebraic quantum mechanics, path-integral quantum mechanics, axiomatic and algebraic QFT, geometric quantization, spacetime algebra, noncommutative geometry, and categorical QFT has been developed across nearly a century of foundational work, with extraordinary depth and rigor. The Heisenberg-Schrödinger-Stone-von Neumann apparatus, the Feynman path integral, the Wightman-Haag-Kastler-Osterwalder-Schrader axiomatizations, Connes’s spectral triples, the Atiyah-Segal-Lurie categorical framework — each is a deep mathematical achievement and is given full credit throughout this paper. The mathematical machinery needed to formalize the dual-channel content of the McGucken Quantum Formalism has been substantially available in prior art: Stone’s theorem (1930), von Neumann’s uniqueness theorem (1931), Feynman’s path integral (1948), and the subsequent operator-algebraic and path-integral literature.

What was missing was the structural commitment to dual-channel content as a foundational feature of quantum theory — the willingness to take the simultaneous presence of algebraic-symmetry and geometric-propagation content in dx₄/dt = ic as a structural commitment of the quantum theory rather than as a coincidence of mathematical formulation. The McGucken Quantum Formalism supplies this commitment, with formal mathematical content (Definitions 7.5.1-7.5.3, Propositions 7.5.1-7.5.4, Lemma 15.1) establishing the categorical novelty as a structural-mathematical fact rather than as a philosophical interpretation.

The structural significance of this commitment is that it supplies the foundational principle from which the entire quantum-theoretical apparatus descends as theorems through dual-channel routes. The Hamiltonian and Lagrangian formulations of quantum mechanics, the Heisenberg and Schrödinger pictures, the wave-particle duality, the locality-nonlocality dual, and the seven-duality structure of physics all become parallel sibling consequences of a single principle, with the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ reached by two disjoint routes through structural overdetermination. The categorical novelty of dual-channel quantum theory is the missing structural foundation that transforms the prior art’s rich apparatus from a collection of equivalent mathematical formulations into a unified derivational program from a single geometric principle.

The companion paper [MG-Geometry] establishes the analogous result on the geometric side: moving-dimension geometry is a categorically novel geometric category not equivalent to any prior framework of dynamical geometry. The two papers together establish that the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic articulates two categorically novel mathematical structures — one geometric, one quantum-theoretical — with the dual-channel content being the formal-mathematical signature of the deeper unity. The principle is geometric in its foundation but generates both differential-geometric and quantum-theoretical content as parallel sibling theorems. This dual generation is what makes dx₄/dt = ic foundational in the deepest sense: it does not specify a category of physics but supplies the foundational principle from which categorical structures of dual-channel objects descend on both sides of the geometric/quantum-theoretical divide.

**In plain language.** Quantum mechanics, quantum electrodynamics, quantum field theory, and the Standard Model have been developed across nearly a century into the most precisely tested theoretical structures in the history of science. Their mathematical apparatus is deep, rigorous, and beautiful, and is given full credit throughout this paper. What no prior framework supplied was a single foundational principle from which both the algebraic-symmetry side (operator algebras, canonical commutation relations) and the geometric-propagation side (Huygens wavefronts, path summation, action principles) descend as parallel sibling theorems through disjoint routes. The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic supplies this principle. The dual-channel content was always mathematically present in the formula x₄ = ict (Minkowski 1908). The willingness to commit to it as a structural foundation of quantum theory is what was missing, and what the McGucken Quantum Formalism supplies. Together with the companion McGucken Geometry paper, the two-paper sequence establishes the McGucken Principle as the foundational geometric postulate from which two categorically novel mathematical structures — moving-dimension geometry and dual-channel quantum theory — descend.
